Rev AD

Objectives
At the end of this training course, trainees will be able to:
l State the safety aspects of the 6530/ 6330 TTO printers operation and installation.
l Install and demonstrate the ability to configure, create messages and change parameters using related software.
l Install and set up Leader- Follower and print sub images.
l Demonstrate the ability to transfer variable data using Text Communication.
l Relate the principles of intermittent and continuous operation on a TTO 53mm printer.
l Align and calibrate the 6530/ 6330 to achieve an acceptable print.
l Carry out basic maintenance as described in the relevant manuals.
l Diagnose common faults associated with the 6530/ 6330 TTO printers.
The main purpose of this course is to provide the trainees with sufficient knowledge to operate the Videojet 6530 TTO Printer
safely and confidently. The trainee should exit the course able to create codes in accordance with customer requirements and
make code setup changes to achieve an acceptable print.

Electrical Hazards
Several possible hazards exist when working with electronics.
l High Voltage (Mains)
l Stored Voltage
l RF Energy
The term high voltage usually means electrical energy at voltages high enough to inflict harm or death upon living things.
Personal Protection from Electrical Hazards
Protect yourself when working with electronics.
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply.
Observe all statutory electrical safety codes and practices. Unless it is necessary to run the printer, disconnect
the printer from the mains electrical supply before removing the covers or attempting any service or repair
activity. Non- adherence to this warning can result in death or personal injury.
l Isolate the power supply
l Check DC Supply
l Keep working area clear of unnecessary personnel

6530 Student Training Guide
l Practice battery handling safety.
l Correctly install batteries to prevent danger of explosion.
l Replace only with the same or equivalent type.
l Dispose of used batteries according to the battery manufacturer’s instructions.
Comply with Lockout/Tagout Procedures.
Do not service electrical parts or circuits without proper lockout/tagout procedures for printer or conveyor
power sources.

Safety
Using A Fire Extinguisher
Only those trained may operate a fire extinguisher. All fires must be reported no matter the size.
Use the Correct Extinguisher Type for the Fire
Select an available extinguisher that matches the type of fire.
l Class A - Ordinary Combustibles
l Class B - Flammable Liquids
l Class C - Electrical Equipment
l Class D - Combustible Metals
Use the PASS Method to Extinguish the Fire.
1. P ull the locking pin.
2. A im at the base of the fire.
3. S queeze and hold the discharge lever.
4. S weep from side to side.
Monitor the Area
Watch the area of the fire incident to be sure the fire does not reignite.
Fire Hazards
Understand fire hazards, how to prevent them, and how to react to fires.
Elements of a Fire
l Oxygen
l Heat
l Fuel
Fire Hazard Terminology
l Flash Point
Minimum temperature at which a liquid will burn. The Flashpoint for Methyl Ethyl Keytone (MEK) 73°F.
13
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
l Flammable Range
Environmental conditions in which there is a fuel/air mixture that leads to combustion, e.g not too lean (too little fuel), not
too rich (too much fuel).
l Lower Flammability Limit (LFL)
The lower limit of the flammable range. Also know as Lower Explosive Limit (LEL).
l Upper Flammability Limit (UFL)
The upper limit of the flammable range. Also known as Upper Explosive Limit (UEL).
l Bonding
Connecting two objects or pieces of equipment with a conductive cable to maintain the same electrical potential between
the two. Grounding
Classes of Fire
Fires are classified as one of four types.
Class A - Ordinary Combustibles
Most common fire. A burning house is a class A fire.
Class B - Flammable Liquids
Liquid combustibles such as paint strippers, solvents, fuels, oil, or grease.
Class C - Electrical Equipment
Additional danger from electrical shock. Power equipment OFF immediately.
Class D - Combustible Metals
Combustible metals such as magnesium, aluminum, and metallic sodium. Metals burn easier in powder or chip form.
DO NOT apply water to a Class D fire as this can result in an explosion.
Fire Prevention & Safety
l Always store flammable liquids in approved cabinets.
Mechanical Hazards
Exercise caution when working with moving machinery.
Moving Equipment Hazards
l Adhere to all posted rules when working around rotating machinery.
l Keep hands, feet, and clothing away from clear of the printer and any moving parts during operation.

U.S., customers should contact a Videojet subsidiary office or their local Videojet distributor.

Shock Hazard Warning
Voltages used to connect the printer to other equipment must not be greater than 50 V dc or peak ac.
Possible Equipment Damage
The device must be switched off when the printheads are being installed, connected or disconnected.
Moving Equipment Hazard
Adhere to all posted rules when working around rotating machinery.
Personal Injury
Keep your hands and clothing clear of the printer while it is on.
Personal Injury
Do not place your fingers under the printhead when the equipment is operating.

Possible Equipment Damage
Failure to adjust the drive belts with the belt tension tool will result in equipment damage. Belt tension shall be
set to 10.
Possible Equipment Damage
The printhead may become hot during normal operation. Observe necessary precautions before attempting to
touch the printhead.

System Overview
The 6530 printer is an airless Videojet Thermal Transfer Overprinter (TTO) that prints high resolution images of data such as date,
time, batch code, ingredients, logos etc. onto flexible film packaging, labels, and glossy cards. The 6530 has a ribbon capacity of
meters and a digital printhead that requires no heating or cooling of the typeface, no mixing of solvents, and produces very clean
print on primary packages.
Printer Description
There are two main parts of the Videojet Dataflex 6530 printer:
l Printer
Houses the printhead and ribbon. The printhead receives data transferred from the controller and then prints onto the
packaging film.
l Clarity Controller Display
Houses the power supply unit and touch screen user interface. You can use the touch screen to access jobs, setup jobs,
and set the various print parameters.
The 6530 printer is an airless Videojet Thermal Transfer Overprinter (TTO) that prints high resolution images of data such as date,
time, batch code, ingredients, logos etc. onto flexible film packaging, labels, and glossy cards. The 6530 has a ribbon capacity of
meters and a digital printhead that requires no heating or cooling of the typeface, no mixing of solvents, and produces very clean
print on primary packages.
The 6530 is equipped with an onboard print detection system called iAssure . This print detection system detects print errors such
as missing print, ribbon creasing, dust, and print placement. This data is displayed in real time as the print is imaged .
Like all Videojet TTO printers this printer uses 2 bi- directional ribbon drive motors that drive the ribbon unwind and rewind reels
which recapture unused ribbon after each print. To enhance the ribbon drive an encoder has been added to ensure ribbon tension
and accuracy within 0.5mm of the last print. Two additional stepper motors drive the printhead on a linear axis and the print -
head in and out. To ensure the proper force is applied to the printhead a second encoder is used. The printer also has the cap -
ability to change between intermittent and continuous operation. The 6530 controller consist of a power supply and a
touchscreen that can be separated. The controller and power supply communications package delivers signals via 1 serial port, 2
Ethernet ports or 2 USB 2 ports.
Videojet TTO printers can be controlled by a host packaging unit's controller using web server or it can be configured into Leader-
Follower Mode controlling up to 4 printers with a single controller.
There are 3 levels of password protect within it security features. There is also an onboard diagnostics system to aid in
troubleshooting.
The Videojet Dataflex 6530 printer uses high- resolution thermal transfer technology with a unique electronic ribbon and print -
head controls, eliminating the need for compressed air. The printer has minimal wear parts reducing the maintenance require -
ments. A quick release printhead design improves production uptime.
19

l Available as a standard 53 mm print width model.

Low Speed Printing
If speed goes below 1mm/sec different actions will be performed depending on parameters set. These are:
Abort print, abort after timeout, resume (when speed increases), retry.

Special Tools
Special tools are needed to set the printhead gap and properly tension the printhead and carriage belts.
SAR 10222
The SAR 10222 gap tool measures the distance/gap between the printhead and the print platen of a TTO printer. The ideal dis -
tance/gap between the print head and print platen is 2 mm. The tip of the tool is 1mm deep. The etched lines represent increasing
depth in 1 mm increments with the first etched line at the ideal 2 mm gap. The maximum gap on the tool is 5 mm.
F IGURE 4- 1: Gap Tool with increments labeled.
21
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Measuring Print Gap
1. Power the TTO printer OFF .
2. Remove cassette from TTO printer.
3. Slide the printhead from its parked position to the home print position.
4. Ensure that the print platen/roller is installed.
5. Use the gap tool to measure the gap between peel roller and platen .
6. The end of the second etched line is the ideal 3.7 mm gap when reading the gap from the peel roller. This will put the print -
head at 2mm.
Belt Tension Tool 407954
Use the tension tool after replacing the carriage motor, printhead motor, pulley or either belt.

6530 Student Training Guide
TTO Ribbon
A thermal transfer ribbon is a consumable which contains the ink required for printing. Though the exterior appearance of TTO
ribbon may be identical, TTO ribbons differ in the properties of the inked ribbon layers (back coating, polyester film (PET), ink lay -
ers) and in all the components which make up the roll (core, ribbon trailer, ribbon leader).
Possible Equipment Damage - Ribbon storage
Print ribbons should be stored at a temperature range of 5°C to 40°C, and at a non- condensing humidity range
of 20% to 85%.
F IGURE 4- 4: Anatomy of TTO Ribbon.
Table 4- 3: Ribbon Linear Components
Part Description
A PET film which protects the inked part of the ribbon during transport and makes insertion into the printer
Leader
easier. It may bear the customers brand name.
The thermal transfer ribbon is made of a PET film upon which a thermofusible ink is deposited on one side
Ribbon
and a protective layer called backcoating on the other side. Depending on ribbon grade, the ribbon structure
varies according to its number of layers, the composition of each layer.
The trailer is situated at the end of the ribbon and plays an important role. The trailer indicates the end of the
Trailer
ribbon which stops the film unwinding, thereby protecting the printhead (depending on the type of printer).
The plastic core is the spool on which the film is wound during manufacture and from which it is unwound
Core
during the printing. Depending on the model of printer for which it is designed, the mandrel will have different
properties (interior and exterior diameter, width, notches, etc.).
24
Rev AD

System Overview
Table 4- 4: Inked Ribbon Layers
Part Description
The back coating is part of the ribbon which is in direct contact with the printers printhead.
Back coating
l Made from silicon.
l Protects the printhead as the ribbon unwinds.
l Provides excellent heat conductivity to enable the ink to transfer to the print medium.
l Reduces the formation of static electricity which is harmful to the printer and it's
operation.
The PET film is the basic medium for TTO ribbon and is the thickest part at 4.5 microns.
Base Film PET
l Provides excellent tear resistance during both manufacturing and printing.
l Ensures perfect heat conductivity.
The primer release layer helps the ink to transfer to the substrate.
Primer/Release
Layer
The Ink layer is made of a mixture of wax, resin, other additives, and color pigments.
Ink
l The composition of the ink gives it very different print characteristics.
l This variability of ink composition is what makes TTO so versatile in its various fields of
application.
The top coat is part of the ribbon which is in direct contact with the substrate. The top coat keeps
Top Coat
the ink from sticking to itself when it’s in roll form.
Wax/Resin Applications
l General purpose ribbon for all speed ranges
l Provides good smudge and scratch resistance
l Good heat resistance up to 100 °C
l Good range of colors available
l Good print quality throughout speed range
Resin Applications
l Provides excellent smudge and scratch resistance
l Excellent heat resistance up to 250 °C
l Smaller range of colors available compared to wax/resin
l Slow speed applications only
l Superior print quality to wax/resin ribbon
25
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
No Wax Only Ribbons
Ribbons with a wax only ink layer are not suitable for TTO applications.
Ribbon Damage
The use of incompatible ribbon can seriously damage your equipment and such damage will not be covered
by your equipment warranty. Use only the ribbon approved by your dealer.
Ribbon Storage
Print ribbons should be stored at a temperature range of 5°C to 40°C, and at a non- condensing humidity range
of 20% to 85%.
Ribbon Core Identification
All Videojet TTO ribbons have a light colored core on which is printer their specific Videojet part numbers and manufacture's lot
number.
F IGURE 4- 5: Ribbon Core Identification
Videojet ribbons deliver the highest possible print quality as they are manufactured in accordance with ISO9000 policies in clean
room environment, which eliminates external contaminants. Plastic cores provide superior grasp over paper on ribbon mandrels
preventing slippage. Ribbon lengths up to 1200 meters reduce the frequency of ribbon changes thus increasing productivity.
The structure of Videojet ribbon part numbers identify the ribbon characteristics as illustrated in Figure 4- 6 on the facing page .

Ribbon Compatibility
Knowing which ribbon is most suitable for your particular application may not be easy. To achieve the best printing performance
from your TTO printer requires an optimum match between your printer, the material on which you are coding, and the TTO rib -
bon.
Possible Equipment Damage
The use of incompatible ribbon can seriously damage your equipment and such damage will not be covered
by your equipment warranty. Use only the ribbon approved by your dealer.

6530 Student Training Guide
Consequences of Using Incorrect Ribbon
If careful consideration is not taken when selecting the right ribbon, the customer may encounter print problems.
l Pinholes are caused by the substrate being too rough or by foreign material under the substrate or ribbon.
l Grey shading is caused by an incompatibility of the ribbon and substrate.
l Partial ink transfer is caused by an incompatibility of the ribbon and substrate.
l Vertical lines in your print is caused by a dirty printhead or damaged printhead elements.
F IGURE 4- 8: Pinholes in print.
F IGURE 4- 9: Grey shading in print.
F IGURE 4- 10: Partial Ink Transfer.
F IGURE 4- 11: Vertical Lines in print.
Replacing the Ribbon
Possible Equipment Damage
The use of incompatible ribbon can seriously damage your equipment and such damage will not be covered
by your equipment warranty. Use only the ribbon approved by your dealer.
1. Remove the cassette.
2. Put the cassette down on a flat surface with the ribbon spools facing upward and the ribbon rollers towards you .
3. Firmly pull the two ribbon spools (one full of used ribbon) from the cassette. You can use the discs that are situated under
each spool to lever the spool off the cassette.
4. Discard the used ribbon and spools.
5. Open a new, shrink- wrapped roll of ribbon and unwind about 12 inches of ribbon (30 cm). The spool holders on the cas -
sette have color coded discs:
l The black disc is for the new roll of ribbon.
l The silver disc is for the empty roll. When the printer is running, the new ribbon unwinds from the black holder and
winds onto the silver holder.
6. Slide the full spool onto the holder with the black disc. The roll should be located such that the ribbon unspools from the
outside of the full roll, as shown in Figure 4- 12 on the facing page .
30
Rev AD

System Overview
Secure the Spool
Ensure that the spool is pushed completely down onto the holder.
F IGURE 4- 12: Replacing the Ribbon, unspooling.
7. Route the ribbon so that it:
l Unspools from the outside of the full roll
l Goes around the outside of the white roller that is nearest to the full roll
l Runs along the bottom of the cassette
l Goes around the outside of the second white roller
l Passes around the outside of the empty spool
31
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
8. Slide the empty spool completely down onto the holder with the silver disc.
F IGURE 4- 13: Replacing the Ribbon, fully routed and mounted.
9. Turn the empty spool by hand to take up any excess ribbon. Stop turning the empty spool when the full spool starts to
rotate.
Ribbon with Leader
If the supplied ribbon has a transparent section or printed leader at the start, rotate the empty spool to
take up all of the transparent ribbon.
F IGURE 4- 14: Newly loaded and advanced ribbon.
10. Replace the cassette into the printer body.
Ensure that:
l The ribbon runs in between the printhead and the printer's print roller or print pad
l The ribbon is not twisted or caught
l The ribbon has not become slack on the cassette while replacing the cassette.
32
Rev AD

System Overview
Temporarily Removing Ribbon
Sometimes you might need to remove a ribbon temporarily (perhaps because you need a ribbon of a different width for a job).
When you put a partly used ribbon reel back into the printer, ensure that:
l You push the unused reel onto the black disc holder.
l You push the partly used reel onto the silver disc holder.
Ribbon Tracking
The following steps should be avoided to prevent ribbon tracking:
l Job placement
l Ribbon webbing
Job placement
Centering the job on the ribbon distributes friction evenly, to prevent tracking issues.
F IGURE 4- 15: Ribbon Tracking
Webbing new ribbon
When using a new roll ensure the leader is properly placed at the bottom of the core.
If the core and the leader are not parallel. Peel the leader from the core and re seat the leader to the core.
33
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 16: Ribbon Leader Gap
In order to prevent the ribbon from tracking, it is important to ensure the ribbon has been installed correctly. Small imperfections
when the silver starter is wrinkled or creased will multiply as the waste ribbon is taken up.
l Pull gently on ribbon and guide as you turn the mandrel.
l Ensure washers are fully seated before webbing the cassette.
l Always insert the cassette with the printer offline.

Printhead
Videojet uses corner edge technology for fast print speeds up to 1000 mm/ second.
Printhead Heat
The printhead may become hot during normal operation. Observe necessary precautions before attempting to
touch the printhead.
Thermal Transfer Theory of Operation
1. When the printer receives a print signal, the printhead is triggered by the printer to apply pressure and heat to an inked rib -
bon (solid at room temperature).
2. The combination of heat generated by the printhead (which melts the ink) and pressure transfers the ink from the ribbon to
the target material.
3. The head presses the substrate against a rubber print platen or roller.
4. The transferred ink cools rapidly and adheres to the target, forming the image.
5. The printhead retracts and waits for the next trigger.
Form and function
A thermal printhead is comprised of a linear array of printing elements, or dots . Each dot is a tiny resistor and the printhead res -
istance is measured as the total resistance of all the dots in the array. This resistance of individual printheads varies from print -
head to printhead within acceptable manufacturing tolerances. The specific resistance of each printhead fitted to a thermal
printer is critical to correct operation because this resistance is a key parameter in the control algorithm the printer uses to decide
how much power to supply to the printhead for quality print.
Many printers require the user to manually enter the printhead resistance value (marked on each printhead) into the interface
each time a new printhead is fitted to the machine. This can lead to data entry error and consequently to poor performance of
the machine. The 6530 prevents risk by automatically measuring each individual dot's resistance and calculating the total print -
head resistance to feed into its printhead control algorithms.
l The printhead is magnetically attached to the printhead assembly for quick disconnection.
l The printhead and printhead ribbon cable are one piece.
l The printhead resistance value is automatically determined by the printer, (no manual entry).
l The printhead has a chip that identifies it as a Videojet printhead, without which the printer will not work.
l Dot Density is 12 dots/mm.
36
Rev AD

System Overview

Printhead Cleaning
When debris build up on the printhead, the heat from the dot cannot completely pass through to the ribbon which results in poor
print quality. Cleaning will restore print quality, but what happens if it is not cleaned? As build- up attracts more build- up and it is
continually baked at over 80 degrees each print. Operators might turn up force and darkness to compensate, making the situ -
ation much worse.
This build- up of debris can now solidify onto the printing surface to such a point where normal cleaning has very little effect. The
result is a printhead that can no longer effectively dissipate its heat energy and will eventually lead to its premature failure. Dis -
colored print elements indicate build up is damaging the printhead. Clean the printhead every ribbon change!
Possible Equipment Damage
The use of an incompatible cleaning kit can seriously damage your printer. Such damage will not be covered
by printer warranty. Use only cleaning kits approved by your dealer.
40
Rev AD

System Overview

Never touch the printhead with sharp objects and never use any cleaner other than approved Videojet cleaning
swabs.
Possible Equipment Damage - Allow Printhead to Dry
Failure to allow the printhead to dry after cleaning the printhead before the printer is powered will result in
damage to the printer.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not use high pressure air or cotton as these will damage to the printhead.

Factors Affecting Printhead Wear & Damage
l Environment (wet, hot, cold, condensation, abrasive contaminants, dust, etc.).
l Incorrect printhead resistance setting.
l Substrates (e.g. paper, paper backed films, labels are coarse and can speed up wear).
l Parameter settings (constant high darkness).
l Printhead cleaning (frequency and solvents used other than Isopropyl).
l General operator care
l High printhead force
l High throughput.
l Long messages.
l Non VJ platen pads or roller
l Non VJ ribbon
Printhead Diagnostics
To check the health of the printhead press Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Printhead Health | Health Graph .
The Printhead Health function scans the printhead four times at start up and then monitors the health of the printhead every 30
minutes. Resistance reads high on the Health Graph if you overdrive the dots.
F IGURE 4- 29: A healthy printhead health graph readout.
Figure 4- 30 on the facing page shows a Health Graph readout for a printhead with dead dots. The top graph with the blue line
shows the average resistance values seen by the 640 dots. The multicolored Resistance Change bar graph at the bottom indicates
the status of the dots of each printhead element. For example, a red line in the graph indicates that the individual printhead ele -
ment resistance is not at the optimal value. The printhead may need to be changed if the print quality is affected.

Table 4- 7: Color Coding of Resistance Change Graph
Color Meaning
Orange Indicates a 10% drop in resistance and likely too much darkness.
Red Indicates that the dot is dead.
Purple Indicates dot is dead but the printer is not using that dot.
Blue Indicates average resistance.
Yellow Indicates the dots is reach the limit of optimal resistance.
F IGURE 4- 30: A health graph with dead dots showing red in the Resistance Change bar graph.

Setting the Printhead Gap
Setting the printhead is important for general and high throughput. During general throughput set the peel roller gap to 3.75mm
from the roller platen or platen pad, using the gap tool (SAR 10222). A 3.75mm gap at the peel roller, will give you a 2mm gap at
the printhead. During high throughput set the peel roller gap at the peel roll to 3mm. The gap can be adjusted by adding or
removing shims for the printer support bracket.
The Print Head Out Distance (ref to bottom of peel roller) for the 107mm printer is 7.5mm. The printhead to platen gap will
change the angle of the printhead.
Table 4- 8: 107mm Gap Chart
Printhead to platen Gap (mm) Print angle Platen gap to peel roller
25.33 3.2mm
1.5
26.37 3.7mm
2
27.43 4.2mm
2.5
28.51 4.7mm
3
29.61 5.2mm
3.5
The gap tool has white line that indicate 2- 5mm gaps.

F IGURE 4- 37: Left 3.75mm gap resulting in 2mm gap on right.
To add in setting the gap 3 different size shims are used, the largest size is 1mm.
F IGURE 4- 38: Shims
To check the printhead gap without the gap tool. Put the printer in print mode and print at less 10 times. Press Tools | Diagnostics
| Printhead | Inputs . The gap distance will be recorded under Printhead to Platen Distance.
49
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 39: Printhead to Platen Distance setting in Clarity .
The nominal gap between the bottom of the peel roller and platen should be 3.75mm to achieve at 2.00mm ‘ready to print’ pos -
ition. If your printer appears to show the peel roller to platen gap somewhere between 7.00- 7.50mm. The biggest cause of this
error is the print head magnet not finding the datum on the carriage in home position. The system works by finding the datum
(home magnet engaged) and counting encoder steps away to our ‘ready to print’ position. If we never get to the datum home pos -
ition because we haven’t engaged the home position magnet – the system will still count the same number of steps away from
whatever point it stops moving (creating a floating datum). The result is the print head coming out further than is should, inac -
curate ‘ready to print’ position and reduced platen gap.
There are three primary reasons why this can happen:
1. The print head is clashing with the peel roller when it is completing its sweep from home position to read to print/print pos -
ition preventing it from finding the correct datum by stalling the motor.
How to diagnose fault: With the printer turned off and cassette removed, push the print head to the home position with
the print head magnet against the carriage receiver (approximately 2mm away from the light box). Move the head by
hand away from the home position magnet and through its printing sweep until you reach the end stop or hit platen. Do
this a couple of times tilting the head both directions to simulate head float. Look to see if the front edge of the print
head hits on the peel roller during the in and out movement.
Action to correct fault: The peel arm needs to be moved to the correct position – Remove print head and pivot pin to split
head pivot and carriage. Loosen belt clamp bolts and remove 4 screws holding carriage to the linear slide. Now you can
access the peel arm mounting screws – ensure the top of the peel arm is parallel with the metal work.
PLEASE NOTE: Do not push the peel arm too far forward otherwise you will produce fault 2 below.
2. Incorrect belt tension preventing the head from parking correctly – home position magnet never mating with carriage cre -
ating ‘floating datum’.
3. The peel arm is too far forward and is clashing with the lightbox causing the carriage motor to stall and print head not to
find datum.
50
Rev AD

System Overview
How to diagnose fault: Power up the unit with the cassette removed. Watch the print head carriage move away from the
lightbox until it finds the home position hall sensor and the accelerate towards the light box. There should be no less
than 0.5mm between the peel roller and the light box (Be careful to look at the peel arm leading edge (closest baseplate)
as this is slightly proud of the peel roller itself). If unsure, stick some masking tape on the edge of the lightbox and see if
the peel arm leaves a witness mark after power up.
Action to correct fault: As per fault 1 but move the peel arm the opposite direction.
How to diagnose fault: Power up the unit with cassette removed. Watch the print head carriage move away from the
lightbox until it finds the home position hall sensor and the accelerate towards the light box. The print head should be lif -
ted in the home position with the print head mount magnet mated to the receiver on the carriage. There should be no air
gap and have flush contact between the two surfaces. Additionally, toggle the print head between home position and
ready to print using diagnostics – outputs and sure it does home every time. Finally, with the unit powered off and cas -
sette removed, manually move the carriage and print head to home position next to the light box. The magnet should
stay attached to the carriage when you remove your hand.
Action to correct fault: Follow belt tensioning procedure on both the carriage and print head belt to ensure both correct.

The device must be switched off when the printheads are being installed, connected or disconnected.
Checking the gap on non Videojet brackets
When measuring a non Videojet bracket, measure from the top of the printer to the platen or roller. The measurement should
exceed 186mm. If the measurement is shorter than 186mm the printer may not have enough gap. This condition could cause
print quality and ribbon pull thru issues.
The following measurement should be take when any of the following conditions:
l When customers have designed their own bracket.
l OEM's brackets.
l When removing and replacing a competitors printer ans leaving their bracket in place.

Cassette
The 6530 cassette does not fit previous versions of Videojet TTO printers. The newly designed 6530 cassette has no clutches
(which means no wearable parts) and also features impact washers that absorb the shock of cassette insertion.
l The 107mm will have 2 upper and 2 lower leaf springs per mandrel.
l The 53 and 32mm will have 3 leaf springs per mandrel.

l The cassette latch assembly is adjustable. Adjust the latch to close the gap between the cassette sensor and the cassette
magnet.
l Cassette rollers guide the ribbon from the new side of the reel to the printing process and then on to the take up reel.
l The black disc indicates the side for new ribbon.
l The silver disc indicates the side for used ribbon.
l The friction cone creates a small amount of friction, holding the ribbon in place during the cassette insertion.
l The mandrels lock into the ribbon drive motors.
l The (3) leaf springs on the mandrels secure the ribbon cores to the mandrels which prevents slippage and tracking issues.

Troubleshooting Cassette Open Faults
If a cassette open fault appears while the cassette is inserted:
1. Check the magnet.
2. Check the adjustment of the latch.
3. Check the condition of the cassette switch.
Inspecting the Cassette
When inspecting the cassette check for the following:
l Worn mandrels
l Damaged mandrel bearings
l Slipping friction disc
l Bend leaf springs
l Improperly installed leaf springs
l Mandrels that bind when turning
l Bent guide pin
l Bent cassette rollers
l Missing magnet
l Cassette latch out of adjustment
Note: Change to the lower Position if you are having tracking issues with 20- 30mm ribbon.
Ensure both disk are leveled. Adjust as needed.
Note: Telescoping of the ribbon will cause iAssure Failures.
For more information on how to change the ribbon the printhead press Tools | Help | Tutorial Videos.

Ribbon tracking will cause iAssure to fault. For best results Videojet recommends using the printer alignment tool to ensure the

l Square the lightbox to the printer frame.
l Ensure the peel roller is square to the lightbox.
l square the printhead to the roller.
l Confirm the printhead gap is within specifications.
l Confirm that the printhead is parallel to the platen roller or pad.

Controller
The controller consists of a removable touchscreen and a power supply unit. The controller has a three PCBs and one 48 volt DC
power supply. The three boards in the controller are the USB riser board, the PSU box PCB, and the GUI board.
Using a Videojet USB 3 the touchscreen and PSU can be placed in a remote location.
61
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 55: Controller parts identified.
Features of the controller:
l TFT 800x480 8” wide screen full- color LCD and touchscreen
l 800MHz CPU speed
l 2 USB ports (500 mA max)
l 2 Ethernet ports
l 1 Serial port (RS232)
l 15 Pin I/O connection, used primary for the encoder.
Each Ethernet port has two LEDs. One represents the connection state (fixed light) and the other represents activity. Connection
state goes on when an active device is sensed at the other end of the cable. Usually the connection state is green or yellow/
orange where green means higher speed and yellow/ orange means lower speed. (Supported speeds are 10/100/1000 mega -
bits/sec). Maximum speed is negotiated at connection, then is fixed. The activity led is usually green and blinks when data is
sensed through the port (either inbound or outbound).
If the LEDs are not functional, check the following:
l PC's IP address.
l Printer's IP address.
l Condition of the Ethernet cable.
l If connecting to a connection box, ensure you are using a straight cable.
l If connecting to a connection box ensure hub has power.
62
Rev AD

48 Volt Power Supply
The 48 volt power supply supplies voltage to the printer PCB only. All other voltages made on the printer come from the printer
PCB.
The 107mm power supply unit will be marked with a 107mm rating label. The 107mm can be used with a 53
or 32mm printer with zero power issues. If a 53 or 32mm power supply unit is used on a 107mm printer and
the job was long enough and there was enough ink being put down the power supply unit could shut down
and then reboot.

Printer
The printer consists of 4 motors, 2 encoders, 4 PCBs and a floating printhead. The encoder location for the 107mm is different
from the 53 and 32mm printer.
F IGURE 4- 75: Printer components identified.
53mm Printer
l First printer in the design line, shares many components with 6330 53mm and the 6330 32mm printers.
l Two 6mm belts drive the printhead and the carriage.
107mm Printer
l Print head / carriage motor pulley, idler pulley and idler pulley shafts increased in width by 4mm.
l To prevent new belt height clashing with print head mechanics the baseplate design modified to allow increased belt width.
l Clamping surfaces of carriage, pivot, PH clamp and peel arm clamp increased to accommodate new belt width.
l Changes to baseplate geometry resulted in motor mount screw positions being moved, resulting in a new motor plate
design.
l New motor plate design accommodates greater forces linear slide.
l Two 10mm belts drive the printhead and the carriage.

32mm Printer
l Only 9 new parts in all (3 handed parts) 95%+ of printer common with the 6530 53mm printer.
l All new parts associated with altering mechanical center line (pivot) for narrower printhead.
Home/Park Switch
The Home/Park Switch senses how far the printhead is from the park position. The location of the home park switch is not hand
specific. A left handed printer will travel to the home park switch located on the right then count backwards to its park position
using the carriage stepper motor.

iAssure
iAssure is an internal print detection system that requires little setup. iAssure tracks missed prints, dust, ribbon tracking, ribbon
creasing, and dead dots. The camera is located near the linear slide and points down at the ribbon.
Videojet Dataflex 6530 iAssure technology reduces waste and rework by conducting print quality spot checks in real time and
detecting recurring print defects.
The printer has a built- in sensor, lens, and light source which creates an image of the printed ribbon waste as the ribbon moves
forward. This captured image is compared to the intended image in real- time, using multiple algorithms to check for areas of mis -
match. These algorithms compare the type and magnitude of mismatch to determine the pass/fail of the code. The printer trig -
gers a fault when a preset number of consecutive codes fail. This fault can trigger a line stop or send the product into a
reject/inspection area (if the line is configured with one).
Remove the Lens Tape
When installing an iAssure equipped printer, be sure to remove the blue shipping tape from the iAssure lens.
F IGURE 4- 100: iAssure Sensor
l Internal print detection system
l Does not require complicated adjustments
l Resolves print quality issues such as ribbon creasing, dust, and printhead element burn out
l Hand specific
l Captures the image produced by the light bar shinning through the ribbon
There are 3 ways for the operator to interface with iAssure from the home screen, even if the operator is locked out of the dia -
gnostic screen do to password restrictions.
94

System Overview
l Consecutive print defects can be viewed.
l iAssure can be viewed by touching the job preview (R06).
l Recommendations can be viewed by touching the iAssurance percentage (R07).
F IGURE 4- 101: iAssure form the home screen
The iAssure can be toggled on or off, which may be necessary to troubleshoot the camera picking up false images or if the cam -
era is damaged.
The iAssure can fault too frequently, thus unnecessarily stopping production. If the printed image looks good, it may become
necessary to increase the number of consecutive failures before faulting.
To enable or disable the iAssure or to change the number of consecutive failures before fault value. Press Tools | Setup | Print -
head To enable or disable iAssure, press iAssure faults enable yes or no. To change the fault value, press iAssure consecutive fail -
ures before fault and enter your new failure value.

iAssure automatic vertical registration (R07) has three modes that can be set in CLARiTY Configuration Manager:
l Off mode - the printer will not make correction to ribbon tracking and will fault when part of the code is missing because of
tracking.
l Move off ribbon mode - iAssure will move the image on the ribbon in 1mm increments in order to correct ribbon tracking.
l Center of ribbon mode - iAssure will center the image on the ribbon. If tracking occurs iAssure will move the image in 1mm
increments to keep the image centered on the ribbon.

Ribbon Position Sensor
The ribbon position graph shows you where the ribbon is sitting on the sensor. We take readings during calibration (about every

of how ribbon tracked. You can literally think of the black region as ribbon sitting on the sensor. So as it moves up/down that is
actual ribbon tracking. Vertical scale represents the Sensor pixel number. When the printer is powered off the graph will be lost.

Loss Data

bar. The test is done with the cassette removed.

Lightbox Alignment
1. Place the printer unit on its back cover with power turned off and cables removed.

pushed.
3. Place the TTO alignment tool between the inside face of the lightbox assembly and the baseplate as shown below. Note:
Note: Be careful the ensure the alignment tool clears the screw head on the side of the lightbox.

two mounting bolts are fully tightened.
5. Reconnect cables and power up coder.
6. Complete iAssure full calibration.

iAssure Calibration
When in the iAssure Wizard mode the distance between prints is different than the normal printing mode. The iAssure Wizard
Setup redetermines the distance between the printed image to the iAssure sensor and recalibrates itself accordingly. The amount
of horizontal registration used is also factored into the distance the ribbon will travel before being captured by the iAssure sensor.
l Run the Reset iAssure Setup procedure anytime something done to the printer can affect the distance, such as installing a
new or rebuilt light box, changing the carriage mechanics,
l Printhead changes do not require using the wizard.
l We currently ship some 55 mm ribbon with the printer which could be used during calibration.
l Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | iAssure | iAssure Reset Calibration.
l The manufactures password is needed to access this feature.
l Use iAssure setup when switching from intermittent to continuous.
l Use iAssure setup when the iAssure preview image is unstable.
l Used iAssure setup to troubleshoot iAssure failures.
l Can be calibrated in CLARiTY configuration manager.
l Prints a series of 10 rectangles, must have good print quality prior to the calibration.
l Ensure ribbon is not tracking prior to calibration.
The iAssure setup wizard now allows setup of the iAssure system with printheads that have dead dots. The following limitations
apply with R06:
l At least 50% of the dots within the printed black box must be working.
l For the full set up to be completed there must be no dead dots within 5mm of either edge of the black box.
l If there are dead dots within this 5mm range only the distance from the Print line to the camera line will be configured.
Since this is typically the only value that needs to be changed on installation – this means the only time a new printhead
will be required will be when any printer PCB is replaced, or when the lens is replaced.
Remove Protective Tape
Failure to remove the protective tape from the iAssure camera will result in a failed calibration of iAssure.
Premium Ribbon
It is not recommended to set the iAssure setup with premium ribbon. Premium ribbon could result in iAssure
failure.

Rev AD

System Overview
iAssure Code Failures
iAssure is a built- in, simplified, real time visual inspection system, capable of detecting major commonly occurring
TTO print defects, without any need for operator setup, offering to reduce waste and rework without being a productivity
liability.
Although the printer prints at 300 dpi and the captured image resolution is 128 dpi, it is not possible to undertake analysis in real
time without affecting throughput. The 128 dpi is used as a visual aid on GUI only. All processing is carried out at a
much lower resolution.
Both the intended image (300 dpi) – we call that the “truth data” - and the captured image (128 dpi) are de- resolved to a
much lower resolution. The results are what the naked eye would regard as a wall of bricks.
F IGURE 4- 123: 300 and 128 dpi
The typical print defects which commonly occur in TTO printing have been categorised into 6 types, to which we have given each
a working name (NOTE WELL: these working names are for reference only and should not be taken entirely literally). These are:
•Dust (dirty dots)
•Misalignment
•Worn Platen
•Overprint (ribbon underfeed)
•Ribbon Crease
•Image Darkness (excessively low or High)
In order to ensure that iAssure satisfies its objective NOT to become a productivity liability (false line stops) these thresholds have
been set purposefully LOW, and chosen through prolonged laboratory qualification testing.
It is important to appreciate this aspect of the iAssure design - it is looking for commonly occurring significant errors, which are
what typically happens. iAssure is notseeking to discover that a B has printed to look like an R, or suchlike, as not only is this
impossible at the low resolutions iAssure algorithms work at, but it is un- necessary, as these are not the typical failure modes of
TTO printing.
Thus it requires a significant error, producing a particularly low score, in order for the algorithm to declare a fail.
115
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
iAssure code failure can be configured to stop the printer and raise a fault or trigger a reject on the 15 pin I/O.
Low Darkness
Operators may select the wrong darkness level, causing the image to appear faded.
iAssure registers a low darkness condition when there is a low light level throughout the whole image. iAssure looks for low dark -
ness over the majority of the image before raising a fault.
F IGURE 4- 124: Low Darkness example image.
Low darkness is a result of not enough ink being transferred off of the ribbon to the substrate or poor adhesion. The job in Figure
4- 126 on the facing page was set at 50%.
F IGURE 4- 125: Ribbon and Substrate printing at 50% darkness.
116
Rev AD

System Overview
Corrective actions:
l Clean the printhead
l Check darkness setting
iAssure dust and dead dot failures
iAssure reads a line of print missing down the image as a dead dot or dust. Dust can get under the printhead or the printhead ele -
ment. iAssure looks for Strips of missing transfer in the transfer image.
F IGURE 4- 126: Ribbon and Substrate after prints with dead dot or dust.
l Clean the printhead
l Check total dead dot count
l Remove any dust or debris from print area
Creasing
iAssure reads areas darker than the unprinted ribbon as being creases. Folded or creased ribbon has 3 folds that light would have
to pass through. Because creased ribbon lets through less light iAssure sees less light and registers a crease.
117
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 127: Ribbon and Substrate after prints with creasing.
Corrective actions
l Check ribbon webbing
l Check cassette rollers
l Check peel roller
Crease Outside the Image
This fault indicates that creases are captured at the sensor but are outside of the transfer area.
Overprinting
Operators can accidentally rewind waste ribbon onto the new ribbon side. Because the ribbon has been transferred once, the
second transfer will have ribbon with missing ink, leaving substrate that has no transferred image.
iAssure registers overprinting when more ink is removed than is needed to make the image.
118
Rev AD

System Overview
F IGURE 4- 128: Substrate after overprinting.
F IGURE 4- 129: Ribbon and Substrate after overprinting.
Corrective actions
l Check to see if ribbon was wound correctly
l Check that all transferred ribbon is on the waste side
Platen Failures
The platen may become worn over thousands of prints in the same place. If an operator moves the horizontal over the damaged
area, missing squares in the print register to iAssure as platen failure.
119
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 130: Ribbon and Substrate after printing on a damaged platen.
Corrective actions:
l Replace the platen
Misalignment
A damaged bracket can cause misalignment.
When the printhead is not square to the bracket, only part of the image prints - usually the middle portion in the middle of the
transfer area. iAssure is looks for a complete image pattern and reads the half/partial image as a misaligned bracket.
Corrective actions
l Verify the bracket is in good working order.
l Verify the printer support bracket T handle is tight.
No Image Failures
A No Image failure indicates that iAssure looked for an image of the current job and no image was present.
Corrective actions
l Verify the current job has information that can be printed.
Vertical and Horizontal Parameters
If the print is offset off the edge of the printhead (by moving the vertical or horzontal registration), iAssure will not fault.iAssure
checks against the actual printed image (Truth image). NOT THE JOB Should the image be offset completely to a point of no
print, clearly that is not correct and iAssure will fault.

Stepper Motors & Encoders
The 6530 has 4 stepper motors and 2 internal encoders. The purpose of the stepper motors is to keep proper ribbon tension, drive
the printhead in and out, drive the printhead left and right in a linear motion.
The purpose of the 2 encoders on the 53 and 32mm printers is to help with precision movements of the ribbon drive and the print -
head in and out.
The purpose of the 2 encoders on the 107mm printer is to help with precision movements of the carriage drive drive and the print -
head in and out.
Not all models of the 6530 and 6330 will have the encoders in the same location. The 6530 107mm printer
will have a encoder on the carriage motor. 6530 and 6330 32 and 53mm printers will have an encoder on the
waste and the printhead stepper motors.
All 4 stepper motors on the 6530 107mm printer are different from the other TTO printers. refer to the service
manual or the parts list for the correct parts.

Ribbon Stepper Motors
The two ribbon drive motors connect to the cassette to drive the ribbon. The ribbon drive motors are identical bidirectional motors
running on 48 volts DC. The ribbon drive motors are responsible for ribbon tension, ribbon advancement, and ribbon take up dur -
ing printing.

To test the motor volts Press Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Inputs | Motor Volts . The voltage should be 48
volts DC.

The ribbon encoder is located on the right or left ribbon drive motor base on the printer hand. It has a different connector than
the printhead in and out encoder.
l Measure the rotation of the motor with a higher resolution than the stepper motor.
l Aids in ribbon tension.
l Ribbon encoder and printhead encoder are different encoders.

l Protected by solid state fuse.
l Capacitive encoder.
l Aids iAssure in capturing the printed image.
l Used a close loop system.

To test the functionality of the ribbon encoder:
1. Press Tool | Diagnostics | Printhead | Inputs | Ribbon Waste Position Sensor .

3. Starting at zero One turn wast motor one full turn, the value should = 16384. 6500 is the max.
Printhead Stepper Motor
The printhead stepper motor drives the printhead in and out, making contact with the substrate and platen. The printhead step -
per motor works with the printhead encoder to ensure the printhead has the right amount of force applied to the substrate and
platen.

Stepper motor troubleshooting
53mm & 32mm Carriage Stepper motor troubleshooting.
To test – Winding A measure between connector Pin 1 and Pin 2 (wire color may change with motor supplier). A good reading
would be 0.7 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.
To test – Winding B measure between connector Pin 3 and Pin 4 (wire color may change with motor supplier). A good reading
would be 0.7 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.
53mm & 32mm Printhead Stepper motor troubleshooting.
To test – Winding A measure between connector Pin 1 and Pin 2 (wire color may change with motor supplier). A good reading
would be 0.7 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.
To test – Winding B measure between connector Pin 3 and Pin 4 (wire color may change with motor supplier). A good reading
would be 0.7 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.

would be 0.6 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.
To test – Winding B measure between connector Pin 3 and Pin 4 (wire color may change with motor supplier). A good reading
would be 0.6 Ohm and bad reading would be <0.1 Ohm (S/C) or high resistance O/C.

Encoder
The encoder tells the printer the speed of the substrate at the printhead. The encoder should be mounted to the platen roller for
best results. The encoder comes with an O- ring, which will need changing when it gets worn. It is recommended that the 6420
TTO encoder not be used on this model because the number of lines will produce low resolution.
Excessive pressure on the encoder tension spring will cause wrinkles in the ribbon.
l Encoder mode: Quadrature
l Encoder number of lines: 3600
l Encoder wheel diameter: 53.05 mm
l Encoder source: 24 volt push pull
l Connected to the controller, low profile cable or the I/O cable.
144
Rev AD

System Overview
F IGURE 4- 162: A 3600 line encoder.
Pulses per millimeter can be determined by multiplying the diameter of the wheel time pi, then dividing that number by the num -
ber of lines written on the encoder.
Diameter X Л (Pi) = Distance per rotation. D X Л (Pi) = 167mm
Videojet has two TTO encoders a 500 line (DF, DF+ and 6320/6420 series printers) and a 3600 line, it is highly recommend that
the 3600 line encoder is used.
500/167=3 pulses per mm
3600/167=21 pulses per mm
Conclusion 3600 pulse encoder has 7 times the resolution of the old encoder.
Ideally, the encoder wheel should contact the substrate, pressing against the print roller. Mounting the encoder against the sub -
strate will give an accurate speed profile. If the encoder wheel is in contact with the print roller only, there is potential for a mis -
match of speeds between the roller and substrate if slippage occurs.
If the print roller is the same width as the mounting bracket assembly, you can use the fixed or adjustable encoder bracket. If the
print roller is cut to a certain length to match a substrate width, you must use the adjustable encoder bracket.
F IGURE 4- 163: Adjustable encoder bracket.
145
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide

F IGURE 4- 164: Special Encoder Bracket.

is set for continuous print mode. Printer will only print when the encoder direction is forward.

Encoder Profile
An Encoder profile is a graph plotted with captured behavior of the packing film as gathered from the encoders. The graph is
also overlaid with a live oscilloscope readout of the machine inputs and outputs. Encoder profiling provides an analytical tool for
146
Rev AD

System Overview
examining applications in which excessive linear speed or acceleration problems have raised doubt over the suitability of the
printer for the application.
The 6530 Encoder profile shows more information than previous TTO models such as motor and motor sensor movement.
Encoder profile can be captured in intermittent and continuous mode. To capture an encoder profile press Tools | Diagnostics |
Printhead | Encoder Profiles . When capturing encoder profiles in continuous print mode set the profile X axis to distance. When
capturing encoder profiles in intermittent print set the profile X axis to time and set the profile type to printer performance.
F IGURE 4- 166: Encoder Profiles menu in the Clarity interface.

l Blue line indicates speed.
l Printer icon indicates print cycle.

l Lock icon abdicates end of update time.

Print Trigger
There are two options for external print triggers, Volt Free Wiring or a Photo sensor.
150
Rev AD

System Overview
l Volt Free Wiring
l Photo Sensor
Volt Free Wiring
l All I\O connections via 15- way D type on printer
l Print Signal
l Volt free
l PNP
The volt free wiring print trigger method is a preferred method because it ties directly into a Form, Fill, and Seal machine.
We use a 24 volt mechanical relay to isolate the two signals because most Form, Fill, and Seal units do not provide enough cur -
rent flow, and this low current can cause the 6530 to malfunction. Using a relay separates the two signals and allows the 6530 to
use its own 24 volt current to provide a print signal.
Figure 4- 171 below illustrates how a relay is connected to provide a print signal to the 6530's 15- pin D- connector.
F IGURE 4- 171: Diagram: Volt free contact wiring.
Connect relay as follows
Form, Fill and Seal unit signal – Negative lead of coils
Form, Fill and Seal unit signal – Positive lead of coils
Wire- 1 from the I/O cable – Connect to Normally Open contacts.
Wire 2 from the I/O cable – Connect to other side of Normally Open contacts.
The relay acts as a switch. When a voltage/signal is present across the coils, it generates a magnetic field is generated that closes
the contacts of the relay. With the relay closed, the 24 volt/current to travels through the contact from the 6530 pins 1 and 2,
causing a print signal. When the voltage and its magnetic field is removed the contacts open, interrupting the print signal.
Photo Sensor
When a photo sensor determines an object is present, it provides an output. A photo sensor can come in two output methods,
NPN (sinking) or PNP (sourcing). The difference between the two is that NPN (sinking) type sensor switches the load (print signal)
to ground whereas a PNP (sourcing) type sensor is switching the load (print signal) to positive voltage. All 6530 TTO printers
require a PNP type photo sensor when using a photo sensor to detect an object.
Photo sensors are use when the film has registration marks.
One 15- pin D- Type (male) connector located on the side of the printer, is dedicated to input and output connections.
151
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
1. Connect the RED wire from the photo sensor to the positive 24V source wire 1 (red)/ pin 1 of the 6530 15- pin D connector.
2. Connect the GREEN wire from the photo sensor to the print signal lead wire 2 (blue)/ pin 2 of the 6530 15- pin D connector.
3. Connect the BLACK wire from the photo sensor to the ground wire 3 (green)/pin 3 of the 6530 15- pin D connector.
You can configure the printer to print on either a positive or negative edge signal. Note: these settings are Configuration Settings
and cannot be set at the Clarity panel. (refer to Clarity Configuration Manager for details).
F IGURE 4- 172: Diagram: Print trigger PNP Print Signal.
Print Signals
There are several ways to print with the 6530. The most common is the use of a photo sensor or a volt free wiring. When the
device closes it connects the printer's 24 volts to the print input.
Continuous Auto Print Triggering allows indefinite continuous motion printing from a single print trigger. Each print is separated
by a distance that you define in Clarity Configuration Manager in the Continuous Repeat Difference parameter. Continuous Auto
Print Triggering is also toggled on in Clarity Configuration Manager. Auto print mode will have one print per trigger.
Continuous auto print triggering is used when the code placement on the product is not critical.
F IGURE 4- 173: Auto Print Trigger making multiple prints on a web.
The print trigger can create multiple prints for one signal through use of the continuous multi print mode. Multi Print mode can -
not be used in conjunction with continuous motion printing. One print signal generates a number of prints, separated by a
defined distance.
Used when one trigger has to be used to print on multiple products.
152
Rev AD

System Overview
F IGURE 4- 174: Continuous Multi Print generating 3 print per signal.
Debounce
Debounce is a feature that prevents false triggers as the substrate film advances. The print sensor debounce is added to the print
signal and is the distance of film that has to advance with the print signal being logically "On" before the sequence of continuous
prints begins. Debounce prevents misprints from multiple erroneous print signals when a sensor or signal is not giving a clean
switch.
F IGURE 4- 175: Printer sensor debounce.
When printing in intermittent mode, the distance the film traveled cannot be measured. To block unwanted signals in inter -
mittent mode the printer sensor debounce must be set in time instead of distance. This parameter sets the time to wait before
accepting a valid print signal input as being logically "On". Debounce in intermittent applications prevents multiple erroneous
print signals when a sensor or signal does not give a clean switch, when the film speed drops to zero, or drops to near zero when
the print signal is issued.
F IGURE 4- 176: Print sensor debounce time.
153
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide

you are going to power the lamp stack with the 6530, ensure the lamp stack is an LED type.

System Overview
F IGURE 4- 178: Lamp stack wiring using external power supply.
Universal Bracket
The packaging industry boasts hundreds of different types and models of packaging machines that provide a vast range of meth -
ods to meet the ever changing consumer demand for fresh and safe packaging. Packaging styles are constantly changing and
this can mean the introduction of new styles of machines or variations on a theme which may operate faster and more effi -
ciently.
Historically, when there was a requirement for contact coders on packaging machines, it has often necessitated a purpose built
solution involving a detailed technical survey.
This “Special Engineering” element has now been dramatically reduced by the introduction of a “Modular” mounting system for
both intermittent and continuous motion applications on bagging and wrapping machines. This allows the local Salesmen and
Field Technicians to specify an “off the shelf” configuration.
Purpose of a Printer Mounting System
l Presents coder to the film in correct orientation. Rigid & immune to vibration
l Provides positional print registration
l Facilitates location of coder accessories
Printer Mounting System Requirements
155
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
It is important to understand the basic criteria for production of a printer mounting system (also referred to as a Bracket).
The bracket must be robustly engineered with sufficient integrity so as to present the printer in the right orientation, without sus -
ceptibility to vibration and with the correct print head clearance.
The bracket must be able to provide print positional registration to cope with the range of package sizes for the particular applic -
ation.
The Operator must have easy access to replenish ribbon and to clean the print head and print pad.
The bracket should not have any adverse affects on the webbing system of the parent machine and designs should always min -
imize drag and unnecessary diversions to the web path.
l Must provide ease of access
l Must not affect film transport of host machine
Anatomy of the Universal Window Bracket
The universal window bracket is the most common bracket used in TTO coding. It meets the requirements of a majority of TTO
installs and is available in intermittent and continuous versions.
F IGURE 4- 179: Intermittent and Continuous Bracket comparitive illustration.
End Plates
The end plates are the windows, they act as the frame
and a opening to remove the cassette. They are used to
fasten the bracket to the OEM machine.
F IGURE 4- 180: End Plate illustration.
156
Rev AD

System Overview
Support Tubes
The support tubes, tie the end plates together and sup -
port the weight of the printer. They also secure the inter -
mittent platen plate to the bracket system. The support
tubes are made out of stainless steel. The support tube
should not come in contact with the film.
F IGURE 4- 181: Support Tubes illustration.
Diverting Rollers
The diverting rollers, divert the film to the TTO printer
and back to it's original film path. The diverting roller
also keeps the film away from the support tubes. A
install could have 1 to 8 diverting rollers, depending on
printer orientation and film path.
F IGURE 4- 182: Diverting Rollers illustration.
Printer Mount
The printer mount holds the printer to the bracket. There
are 2 basic types of mounts, fixed and swivel.
F IGURE 4- 183: Printer Mount illustration.
Mounting Plates
The mounting plates provide a method to mount the
bracket to the OEM machine or the registration bracket
to the universal bracket. The are threaded holes to allow
mounting on any side both inside and out.
F IGURE 4- 184: Mounting Plates illustration.
Mounting to OEM Machines
Fastening the bracket to the OEM machine can be done in many ways. The universal window bracket comes with (3) thread M6
(6 mm) mounting holes an all 4 sides of the end plate.
157
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
The bracket can be mounted directly to the frame of the host machine or attached to mounting blocks which then attach to the
frame or other structure. The mounting blocks are inclued with the bracket.
F IGURE 4- 185: Example of bracket in an OEM frame.
F IGURE 4- 186: Using mounting blocks to the OEM Machine.
Cantilever mounting is required when only one side of a TTO bracket can be fastened to the OEM machine. Only one printer
should be mounted to a cantilever bracket. The bracket should not exceed 24 inches .
F IGURE 4- 187: Cantilever Mounting
158
Rev AD

System Overview
Film Path Options
The universal bracket has holes for the roller, tubes, and mounting.
F IGURE 4- 188: Universal Bracket end plates with holes identified.
Continuous Film Path
The diverting rollers in this position guide the film enter -
ing the printer around the continuous roller. Altern -
atively, the film can wrap around the roller without need
for diversion. this illustration shows 3 possible film paths
for continuous printing.
F IGURE 4- 189: Continuous Film Path, illustrated.

Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Intermittent Film Path Underneath
The diverting rollers in the position guide the film enter -
ing the bracket from underneath the printer. When the
correct roller are used the fill will be flat across the
printer platen.
F IGURE 4- 190: Intermittent Film Path Underneath, illus -
trated.
Intermittent Film Path Over
The divert rollers in this position guide the film entering
the bracket from above the printer. These rollers can be
used in combination with top rollers to guide the film
around other obstructions if present.
F IGURE 4- 191: Intermittent Film Path Over, illustrated.
L Bracket
The L bracket is available in intermittent and continuous versions. The L- Bracket is slightly smaller in size which makes it an excel -
lent alternative to the universal bracket when space it limited.
l The TTO printer attaches to an L block printer mount.
l The end plate has only 2 sides, mounted adjacently to form a 90° degree elbow.
l The L bracket comes with (3), threaded #M6 (6 mm) mounting holes on only 1 side of the end plate.
l The L bracket must be supported on both sides. It cannot be cantilevered.
l The L bracket mounts directly to the frame of the host machine.
160
Rev AD

l This bracket cannot accept film coming from above in intermittent mode.

roller.

Narrow End Plate Bracket
l The Narrow End Plate bracket measures only 8.7 inches wide, compared to the Universal Bracket, which measures 12.2
inches wide.
l It is designed to fit into areas where the Universal Bracket is too large.
l It is available in intermittent & continuous versions.
l The Narrow end plate bracket comes with (3), threaded M6 (6 mm) mounting holes on 4 sides of the end plate.
l The narrow end plate bracket can be cantilevered up to 24 inches.
The end plates on this bracket are solid. The cassette cannot be accessed through the bracket. The operator must reach around
the end plate to replace the ribbon.

The Databases page provides control over the jobs database of the coder.

F IGURE 5- 7: The Databases menu in the Clarity interface.

External Databases
You can transfer jobs designed in Clarisoft to a printer's internal memory directly from a USB device.
1. Create the job in Clarisoft . See "Creating a New Job" on page 189
2. Save the job to a USB memory device. See "Loading Job to a USB Drive" on page 232
3. Insert the USB memory device to the printer's USB port.
4. Select Tools | Databases | External to transfer jobs to the printer's internal database.

Printhead

F IGURE 5- 8: The Setup Printhead menu in the Clarity interface.
The Printhead page, in intermittent mode, allows you to access the following parameters:
l Horizontal Registration: The position of the print in the printers overall printing window, measured in mm across the width
of the printhead. It enables you to position the print along the width of the printhead.
173
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 5- 9: Horizontal registration
l Vertical Registration: The position of the print in the printers overall print window, measured in mm in the direction per -
pendicular to the printhead.
F IGURE 5- 10: Vertical Registration
l iAssure: Allows the user to enable or disable the iAssure feature.
l Print Delay: The delay between the print signal and the commencing of the print. Measured in milliseconds, it allows
time for the packaging film to come to a complete rest before the printhead comes in contact. It should be set to the
smallest possible value that gives a good quality print.
l Print Speed: The target printing speed of the head in mm/sec. It should be set to the lowest speed that allows com -
pletion of the print in the available dwell time of the packaging film.
l Print Darkness: The amount of energy being transferred into the printhead. It should be set to the lowest value that
provides acceptable quality of print at the required throughput.
174
Rev AD

Clarity Interface
l Print Force: Allows the user to set/ adjust the force to be applied while printing.
l 32mm is from 1.0 to 2.5kg default 1.2kg
l 53mm is from 2.0 to 3.5kg default 2.0kg
l 107mm is from 2.0 to 6.0kg default 3.5kg
l Ribbon Saving Mode: This mode enables a choice of either Interleaved Images, Radial Printing or Intermittent Strips.
l Interleaved Images enables a lower quality draft mode of printing with a 50% saving in ribbon consumption. Not
for use with iAssure.
F IGURE 5- 11: Interleaved Printing
l Radial Printing enables multiple prints to be printed across the width of the ribbon. The number of prints is auto -
matically calculated by the system depending on the image height and the ribbon width.
F IGURE 5- 12: Radial Printing
l The vertical non- printed spaces between each printed field is reduced to 0.5mm. There can be a maximum of 5
fields per job, each field separated by a non- printed Image Separator.

Clarity Interface
Setting the Print Position (Continuous)
We will only review the settings that are different in continuous mode.
When printing continuously, the printhead will press the target material down against the roller, as shown below. This angle
affects the print quality. If the angle is not suitable, the resulting print may appear faded.
F IGURE 5- 15: Setting the Printhead Position.
To find a suitable printhead position, proceed as follows:
1. Touch the Tools button on the home page.
2. Touch the Setup button on the tools page.
3. Touch the Printhead button to open the printhead setup page.
4. Touch the Print Position button to open the print position parameters setup page.
5. Enter a new value using the keypad.
Print Position
You should change the print position parameters in steps of 1 mm. The print position parameter is in 0.01 mm
units, so a change of 100 units will move the printhead by 1 mm. Examine the sample prints from each pos -
ition until you find the optimum print quality at the required print speed.
Touch the Home button to return to the home page.
Usage Note
This feature can be applied only on continuous mode applications.
177

Clarity Interface
Internationalization
The Internationalization menu allows you to set the language of the Clarity interface and the international region, which controls
date and time formats displayed within Clarity .
Setting the Clarity Internationalization
To set the language displayed by Clarity , proceed as follows:
1. Touch the Tools icon on the home page.
2. Touch Setup on the tools page.
3. Touch Control .
4. Touch and select Internationalization from the list. The current language and region is displayed.
5. Touch Region . A list of available regions is displayed.
6. Touch to select your region from the list (all the currently available regions will be listed) and touch OK .
7. Touch Language . A list of available languages is displayed.
8. Touch to select your language from the list (all the currently available languages will be listed) and touch OK .
9. Touch the Home button to return to the home page. The date format displayed in the home page changes according to
the region selected.
Recalibrate Touchscreen
The Recalibrate Touchscreen function allows you to recalibrate the touchscreen if touching the screen does not accurately locate
the correct Clarity button or Icon. The printer requests the user to touch several crosses which are displayed on the screen, one
after the other. The screen is re- calibrated when the automated process is complete.
Calibration Problems
If the calibration of the machine has too many errors and does not allow a user to navigate to this screen via
the Clarity panel, the same functionality can be triggered from within Clarity Configuration manager.
Set Screen Orientation
The Set Screen Orientation function rotates the entire display 180 degrees for when the Clarity panel is installed in an inverted ori -
entation.
Setting the Screen Orientation
Depending on the position in which the CLARiTY controller is mounted, it may be necessary to rotate the screen image by 180
degrees. To change the screen orientation, proceed as follows:
1. Touch the Tools button on the home page.
2. Touch the Setup button on the tools page.
3. Touch the Control button on the setup page.
4. Touch Set Screen Orientation from the list. The Screen Orientation page appears.
179
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
5. Select 0 or 180 degrees depending on your requirement and touch OK. The screen orientation changes.
6. Touch the Home button to return to the home page.
Date and Time
The Date and Time featuer allows you to set the system date and time of the coder.
Setting time and date
1. To set the time and date in the CLARiTY system, proceed as follows:
2. Touch the Tools button on the home page.
3. Touch the Setup button on the tools page.
4. Touch the Control button.
5. Touch and select Date and Time from the list.
6. Touch Date to set the Date, and the calendar page will appear.
7. Select the current month and year using the + and – keys.
8. Touch today's date to select it.
9. Touch OK to save the settings.
10. Touch Time to set the time. The time set page appears.
11. Use the + and – keys to give a value for each of the settings [Hours, Minutes and (optionally) Seconds].
12. Touch OK to save the settings.
13. Touch the Home button to return to the home page.
Communications
The Communications menu allows resetting of all serial communication reports in the event that their setup has become cor -
rupted.

instead of December 25th, even when your calculation would normally produce December 25th.
