Secondary Barcode Symbologies
Different barcode symbologies have different attributes and usages.
Code 128
Code 128 is a very high- density barcode symbology (a special version of it called GS1- 128 is used extensively world wide in ship -
ping and packaging industries). It is used for alphanumeric or numeric- only barcodes
EAN128
EAN128 uses normal Code 128 barcodes, but formats the data in a standardized way to identify the type of information con -
tained in the barcode.
Interleaved Two of Five (ITF)
ITF is mainly used as a standard distribution code printed on corrugated cardboard boxes.
The ITF barcode symbology can store a large amount of information and has the following characteristics:
l With the same number of digits, the size of barcodes can be made smaller compared with other codes. It is effective in fit -
ting barcodes into tight spaces.
l Compared to other codes, more information can be stored (the number of digits can be increased) within the same size
code.
l The bar width can be increased keeping the code the same size and using the same number of digits. Codes with wider bars
allow barcode readers to read the barcodes much more easily, enabling reads from longer distances.
PDF417
PDF417 is a stacked linear barcode symbol format used in a variety of applications, primarily transport, identification cards, and
inventory management. PDF stands for Portable Data File.
GS1 Data Bar Expanded
The Expanded version of GS1 Data Bar encodes the 14 digit GS1 item identification plus supplementary AI elements.
The Stacked version is a variant which is stacked in two rows, either as a truncated version (above) used for small item marking or
as an Omni- directional version (below) designed to be read by Omni- directional scanners.
Primary Barcodes
219
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
To add primary barcodes to your images:
1. Double click an existing Primary Barcode field to open it.
- OR-
1.1. Click the barcode icon in the Clarisoft Toolbox.
1.2. Click in the Job Creation Box in the location where you want the barcode. The Select Barcode Symbology window will
open.
1.3. From the Select Barcode Symbology screen, select the required Barcode type.
Barcode Values
The values used for barcodes of this type are strictly controlled. If not sure on the type of barcode
to be used, refer to the European Article Numbering Association (EAN) or the Uniform Code Coun -
cil (UCC) for more information.
2. Enter the required information and options in the Barcode Tab.
3. Select the General Tab and enter all required information and options.
4. Click Apply to save changes.
5. Click OK .
Primary Barcode Symbologies
Different barcode symbologies have different attributes and usages.
220
Rev AD

Clarisoft
EAN8
EAN8 is a smaller and shortened version of the EAN code. Again the numbers are assigned by the country’s GS1 organization.
EAN8 requires 8 digits (7 if the check digit is calculated automatically) and supports 2 and 5 digit supplementaries.
EAN13
EAN13 is the main scheme used throughout world- wide for retail article numbering. It is a numeric only coding scheme. The >
symbol in the right margin is a light margin indicator. In the left margin the first code digit is used as the margin indicator. No
other marking should appear in the light margins. EAN13 codes require 13 digits (12 if the check digit is calculated auto -
matically).
UPCA and UPCE
The UPC (Universal Product Code) is widely used in the USA as a retail code. However, it has wider applications and this can res -
ult in some confusion.
The actual UPC code is a 10 digit code. The 10 digit number is preceded by a “number system” digit, which is 0 for the retail ver -
sion, and followed by a check digit. In many retail systems only the 10 digits of the UPC code need to be entered in the event of a
mis- scan, so there have been times when the leading 0 has not been included in the human readable form. However, other values
of the number system digit are used for specific purposes (eg. 6 or 7 are used for manufacturing identification numbering, 3 for
drug products, etc.).
The UPC- A code is one variant of a number of 12 digit codes widely used in the USA. Retail codes are usually thought of as those
with 10 digits (or 11 if the check digit is being entered explicitly), and in fact are 12 digit codes made up of a leading 0, followed
by 10 product digits and 1 check digit.
UPC- E codes are shortened versions of UPC- A symbols designed for use in restricted spaces.
Code 39
Code 39 is by far the most common barcode scheme outside the retail area and is read by most scanners.
EAN13
EAN13 is the main scheme used world- wide for retail article numbering. It is a numeric only coding scheme. The > symbol in the
right margin is a light margin indicator. In the left margin the first code digit is used as the margin indicator. No other marking
should appear in the light margins.
EAN13 codes require 13 digits (12 if the check digit is calculated automatically). Numbers used for EAN article numbering are
known as GTIN (Global Traded Item Numbers) assigned by the country’s GS1 organisation. All EAN/UCC 13 barcodes use a
checkdigit based on a Mod 10 calculation.
GS1- DataBar (formerly DataBar RSS)
The GS1- Databar Symbology (formerly RSS) is a family containing three linear symbologies and three stacked variants.
CLARiSOFT only supports 5 variants.
221
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
l The Omni- directional variant encodes the full 14 digit GS1 item identification in a linear symbol that can be scanned in any
direction.
l The Limited version encodes item identification suitable for use on small items, but not for use at point- of- sale.
l The Expanded version encodes the 14 digit GS1 item identification plus supplementary AI elements.
l The Stacked version is a variant which is stacked in two rows, either as:
l a truncated version used for small item marking, or
l as an Omni- directional version designed to be read by Omni- directional scanners.
DataMatrix
DataMatrix is a two dimensional matrix symbology which is made up of square modules arranged within a finder pattern.
DataMatrix symbols may be square or rectangular.
MicroPDF417
MicroPDF417 is a multi- row symbology based on PDF417 designed for applications requiring a greater area efficiency but lower
data capacity than PDF417. A specific and limited set of symbol sizes is available, each size including a fixed level of error cor -
rection.
MicroPDF417 provides for three encoding modes: Text, Byte and Numeric compaction. Text is for general text, Numeric for
encoding data consisting only of digits, and Byte to allow for the first 127 ASCII characters but with a reduced level of efficiency.
Used for tracking parts in vehicle manufacturing, QR Codes are now used in a much broader context, including both commercial
tracking applications and convenience- oriented applications aimed at mobile phone users (known as mobile tagging).
QR Codes
QR Codes storing addresses and URLs may appear in magazines, on signs, buses, business cards, or just about any object that
users might need information about.
Sup 5 Digit
5- digit supplemental bar codes are used on books to indicate a suggested retail price.
The first digit of the supplement indicates the currency in which the price is expressed. A "0" represents a price expressed in British
Pounds whereas a "5" represents a price expressed in U.S. dollars. The remaining 4 digits of the supplement indicate the price. For
example "51195" indicates a suggested retail price of US$11.95 (US Dollars).
Sup 2 Digit
2- digit supplemental bar codes should only be used with magazines, newspapers and other such periodicals. The 2- digit sup -
plement represent the issue number of the magazine. This is useful so that the product code itself (contained in the main bar
code) is constant for the magazine such that each issue of the magazine doesn't have to have its own unique bar code. Never -
theless, the 2- digit supplement can be used to track which issue of the magazine is being sold, perhaps for sales analysis or
restocking purposes.
222
Rev AD

Clarisoft

Connecting to the printer
There are two ways to connect to the printer: serial and TCP/IP. To avoid download failures, ensure that all other Clarisuite pro -
grams are closed before sending jobs to the printer.
Connect via the RS232 Serial Port
1. Select Tools | Options .
2. Select the Printing Tab .
3. In Communication Settings, select the Use Serial Port Communication radio button.
4. Select the from the Port drop- down selector the port to which your serial cable is attached.
Only Available COM Ports Are Displayed
RS232/COM ports already in use by other devices are not displayed.
5. Configure settings to match those set at the printer.
5.1. Select the appropriate Baud rate from the Baud Rate drop- down menu.
5.2. Select the appropriate Parity from the Parity drop- down menu.
234
Rev AD

Clarisoft
5.3. Select the appropriate Data Bits from the Data Bits drop- down menu.
5.4. Select the appropriate Stop Bits from the Stop Bits drop- down menu.
5.5. Select the appropriate Handshaking from the Handshaking drop- down menu.
6. Click Apply to save changes.
7. Click OK .
Connect via TCP/IP
1. Select Tools | Options .
2. Select the Printing Tab .
3. In the Communication Settings group:
3.1. Select the Use TCP/IP Communication radio button.
3.2. Enter the printer IP Address .
3.3. Type the appropriate port number (3001).
Port Number
The TTO printer uses port number 3001.

6530 Student Training Guide
4. Click Apply to save changes.
5. Click OK .

Rev AD

Clarity Configuration
Manager
As coding and labelling equipment have become increasingly versatile and flexible with a wide range of applications, the number
of variables that can be configured within a printer, has become very large. Although printers are pre- programmed with default
values, as the extent of the application increases, it becomes less likely that the default configuration is ideal. This can lead to a
large and cumbersome Menu Tree on the printer’s HMI display that users have to work with.
Most printer variables are set during the installation process. The variables are set to values that tailor the printer to the applic -
ation. Once set, these variables only need to be changed when the application for the printer changes. As such, there is no need to
make these installation parameters available at the printer interface. They are set through a configuration program called Clarity
Configuration Manager .
The Clarity Configuration Manager is a PC software program, that provides the following basic features:
l Setting all the printer variables
l Saving/retrieving a set of variable values to a PC file for later/repeated use
l Downloading a set of variables for non- volatile (permanent) memory storage in the printer
l Uploading a set of variables from the printer for review/comparison/modification
l Updating the system software
l Saving/retrieving language files
l Saving/retrieving job, font, and graphics files
l Configuring Leader/Follower functionality
l Snapshot of Clarity screens
Connecting
To manipulate settings on a printer using Clarity Configuration Manager , you must connect the PC/Laptop running Clarity Con -
figuration Manager to the printer. You can make this connection over a serial port using a null modem cable or over TCP/IP with
an Ethernet cable. The Ethernet connection may be across a single cable or over a network.
Connect via Null Modem Cable
To connect the Clarity Configuration Manager to the printer using an RS 232 connection proceed as follows:
1. Close Clarisoft if it is open.
2. Ensure that all other programs that use the serial port are disabled.
3. Connect the PC serial port to the Clarity Controller using a null- modem cable .
4. Run Clarity Configuration Manager on the PC.
237
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
5. Ensure that the printer status panel (at the bottom left of the window) reports the message "Connection Active" and the
new printer icon turns green.
Status displays "Not connected"
5.1. Disable or quit the other applications running on the PC that are using the serial port.
5.2. Check that the baud rate settings in Clarity Configuration Manager matches the printer’s baud rate.
6. Click the Upload button. The progress of the operation is displayed in the status pane. This uploads the printer parameters
set to the PC.
The list of folders containing the configuration parameters appears in the parameter listing (in the right hand frame of
the Configuration Manager).
Connecting via an Ethernet Cable
Connecting the printer to Clarity Configuration Manager via Ethernet requires the following steps:
1. Correctly configuring your PC Ethernet port.
2. Connecting to the printer with Clarity Configuration Manager .
The PC Ethernet port must be set up to connect at a specific IP address. If you are connecting via an office LAN, consult with
your IT manager before assigning IP addresses to your PC. If you are making a direct connection, you may specify any IP
address.
1. Verify the printer IP Address and that Binary Comms Network Port parameter is set to 3001.
1.1. Select from the Clarity interface Tools | Diagnostics | Control | Communications | TCP/IP.
1.2. Note the IP Address currently assigned to the printer and the Binary Comms Port.
2. Configure the Ethernet port on your PC/laptop.
2.1. Select Start | Control Panel and set View to Small Icons.
238
Rev AD

Clarity Configuration Manager
2.2. Select Network and Sharing Center .
2.3. Select Change adapter settings from the left pane.
2.4. Right click Local Area Connection and select Properties from the context menu.
2.5. Select Internet Protocol Version 4 (TCP/IPv4) from the list of protocols and click Properties .
2.6. Click to toggle on the radio button for Use the following IP address .
2.7. Set the IP address to an available address in the same subnet as the printer, e.g. if the printer IP address is
192.168.1.10 then set this IP address to 192.168.1.x where X is any value between 1 and 255 except 10.
239
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Printer IP Address
The printer IP address should not be the same as the IP address of the PC.
2.1. Set the Subnet mask to 255.255.255.0.
3. Click OK to apply changes and close the window.
4. (In Clarity Configuration Manager ) Click the New printer icon and change the method of connection to Network.
5. Enter the correct IP address and Port number to match the values downloaded into the printer.
6. Click Apply to activate the new settings. The printer icon should turn green.
7. Click the Upload button. The progress of the operation is displayed in the status pane. This uploads the printer parameters
set to the PC.
The list of folders containing the configuration parameters appears in the parameter listing (in the right hand frame of
the Configuration Manager).
Saving Changes to the Printer
To make the changes take effect, proceed as follows:
1. Click the printer icon in the left- hand pane to open the printer controls at the bottom pane of the window.
2. Click the download button to update the printer with the changes that you have made to the parameters. A dialog box
opens with the message “The parameters to be downloaded have not been saved to an archive”.
3. Click OK to continue with the download. The new parameters are active when the download is complete.
Note
Unless steps 1 - 3 are performed, none of the parameter changes are applied at the printer.
Archiving Parameter Sets
You can save the current set of parameters as an archive and stored them on the PC running Clarity Configuration Manager . You
can later load this archive into Clarity Configuration Manager and download the archived parameters back to the printer to
revert to the previous settings.
Once you have completed configuring a printer, it is recommended that you upload the parameters to Clarity Configuration Man -
ager and save them as an archive.
To archive the current parameters, proceed as follows:
240
Rev AD

Clarity Configuration Manager
1. Right click the Archives folder in the left- hand panel.
2. Select New Folder from the context menu.
3. Name the folder.
4. Click Apply .
5. Left click on the folder you created
6. Click New Save in the context menu.
7. Enter a name into the Archive Name field.
8. Click OK . A file for the new save will display under the Archives folder with a name containing the current time and date.
9. Change the name of the archive in the bottom panel if you need to.
10. Click Apply to save changes.
241
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Loading a Saved Archive
To load a previously saved archive, proceed as follows:
1. Expand the Archives folder.
2. Expand the desired folder.
3. Locate the archive in the left panel and right click on it.
4. Choose Load from the pop- up menu. The archived parameters are displayed on the right- hand pane.
Passwords
Toggle the Enable Passwords function to enable or disable password protection. CLARiTY has five different functions that may
be optionally password protected:
l Job Select
l Registration
l Set- up
l Diagnostics
l Databases
F IGURE 7- 1: The Enable Passwords toggle.
Password control can be set up as per the user requirement. Once a user logs into the printer with a password, that password level
remains active until logged out by the user or until it times out.
l Standard (normal) Passwords: This is a general password setup where the printer function is protected by a common pass -
word for the level of user.
l Advanced Passwords: This is a fully configurable option that allows individual users to be setup for bespoke access either as
an individual or part of an access group with individual passwords.
242
Rev AD

Clarity Configuration Manager
If enabled, the 3 levels of passwords must be set up using the following 9 parameters.
l Level 1: Mask Check which functions are to be available to Level 1 users
l Level 1: Name The name of a level 1 user e.g. Operator
l Level 1: Password The password for level 1 (numeric only) User Interfaces Passwords
l Level 2: Mask Check which functions are to be available to Level 2 users
l Level 2: Name The name of a level 2 user e.g. Supervisor
l Level 2: Password The password for level 2 (numeric only)
l Level 3: Mask Check which functions are to be available to Level 3 users
l Level 3: Name The name of a level 3 user e.g. Technician
l Level 3: Password The password for level 3 (numeric only)
Advanced Passwords
Clarity Configuration Manager has a default administrative password. This protects certain parameters from change. To change
the administrative password navigate to Advanced passwords | Change Admin Password. The Default password is password.
F IGURE 7- 2: Advanced Password Screens.

Adding, Editing, or Removing User Groups
User Groups allow you to do just that - group users. You can later set security settings for the entire group. For instance, you have
ten operators that should all have unique user accounts but have the same level of security permission, you can create an Oper -
ators group, put all 10 people in that group, then assign security levels to the group once. All ten people will then have the same
level of access. Future changes to the group only need to be made once, not ten times.

ribbon saving mode 0. None. 1. Interleavened Images – Alternate printhead
Mode 1: Interleaved
dots are activated for each print: odd- evenodd- even and so on interleaving
images
each print with the previous print, thereby doubling the effective ribbon length.
2: Radial
Each print will appear lighter than with no ribbon saving mode and this option
printing

3: intermittent
across the full width of ribbon therefore; If for example a 53mm printhead is
strips
printing a job that is only 9mm wide then with Radial Printing five of these jobs
((9mm + 1mm)x5 = 50mm) will be printed across the ribbon before the ribbon
advances. 3. Intermittent Stripes – Does not apply to Continuous applications.
Utilized where there is a job being printed that has a large space in- between
fields. This space would normally equate to unwanted waste ribbon. With
Intermittent Stripes this large space can be filled with an ‘Image Separator’ (See
Appendix) and the printer will print two smaller prints (without wasted ribbon) as
opposed to one larger print (with wasted ribbon).

Rev AD

Leader- Follower
Applications
Leader- Follower capability is an enhancement that allows you to control up to four printers from a single Clarity Controller .
The Leader- Follower network is typically used when:
l Two or more printheads are required to code onto the same side packaging film
l Control for multiple printers is required from a single location.
Connections
To avoid communications issues, do not connect the printers together until they have faulted out with Leader
absent and Follower absent.
Wiring
Printers should be wired before attempting to configure Leader- Follower.
The following features will not be visible from the Leader and will require the customer to view these features on the individual
Follower printer:
l Availability
l iAssure viewing the latest image
l Event logs
l Production Audit Log
Leader- Follower Wiring
The method you use to wire your Leader and Follwer (s) will differ depending on the number and needs of the application.
Peer to Peer (two printers, no network connection)
When only two TTO printers (one Leader, one Follower) are required and there is no requirement to connect the Leader- Follower
group to an external network:
l Directly Connect the two printers' Ethernet ports with a crossover data cable.
l Connect all other cables (e.g. I/O cable) just the same as the stand- alone printers.
301
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Printer Connection without Box (network connection
required, two or more printers)
The 6530/6330 have the capability to support up to four Videojet supplied TTO printing devices with a single Videojet supplied or
OEM supplied HMI unit so that the user interaction can be simplified on dual filler VFFS or multilane thermoform units. The
6530/6330 printers do not require a junction box, but instead offer two Ethernet ports and an Ethernet Switch to enable daisy
chaining. The Junction box can be used when multiple terminals are needed but is not required.
F IGURE 9- 1: Leader- Follower without Junction Box
Printer Connection Box (network connection required or
more than two printers)
A Printer Connection Box , complete with Ethernet patch cables, is required when more than two printers are to be connected
together or connection to a network is required.
The Connection Box connects up to four TTO printers (one Leader and three Followers). Connected printers can share common
encoder and print signals as well as providing Ethernet connectivity via the integral network switch.
Figure 9- 4 on page 305 illustrates how to wire a Connection box.
302
Rev AD

Leader- Follower Applications
F IGURE 9- 2: Leader- Follower with junction box
Possible Equipment Damage
Connecting printers with power applied will result in equipment damage. Ensure printers are unplugged during
the wiring of the connection box.
Possible Equipment Damage
Equipment damage will occur when I/O wires are improperly connect. To avoid equipment damage refer to
your connection box wiring diagram.
303
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide

Rev AD

Leader- Follower Applications
F IGURE 9- 4: Connection Box Diagram
Configure a Follower Printer
To configure the Follower printer, proceed as follows:
1. Connect your laptop/PC to the Follower printer with an Ethernet cable.
2. Establish communication between Clarity Configuration Manager and the printer.
305
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
3. Right click on the New Printer icon when the icon turns green and select Set as Slave from the context menu.
A Configure Slave Printer dialog box will open.
306
Rev AD

Leader- Follower Applications
4. Select the Slave Number (One, Two, or Three).
Start at One and Number Consecutively
All Follower printers in the group must have consecutive designations starting with One (e.g. having
two Followers numbered One and Three is not permitted).
No two Followers connected to the same network may share the same number.
5. Select an option for Action if connection to Master is lost . This indicates the Leader printer has stopped responding to the
Follower. The options are:
l Ignore the condition
l Raise a warning and continue operation
l Raise a fault and suspend operation.
6. Click OK to program the settings into the printer.
Once the Follower printer is configured, on a Standard TTO Printer, the JOB button on the Clarity homescreen is disabled
(greyed out).
After a short period of time, the follower printer may raise a Warning or Fault message (depending on the setting chosen
for Action if connection to Master is lost ) to indicate that the Leader printer is absent.
Next Steps
1. If the Leader is not yet configured, configure it now.
2. Repeat the above steps to install the remaining Follower printers in the group.
3. With communication established between printers, you can then clear the warning message from the Follower printer
screens.
Configure the Leader Printer
1. If you do not already have the first Follower printer configured, do so now before proceeding with configuring the Leader
Printer.
2. Connect Clarity Configuration Manager to the Leader printer. Right click on the coder icon when it turns green and select
Set as Master from the context menu.
307
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
The Configure Master Printer dialog box will open.
3. In the Slaves Group , toggle ON a box for each follower that you will connect. The dialog box will only allow the selection of
consecutively- numbered follower starting with Slave 1 .
4. Enter into the Name boxes an identifier for the Leader and each follower printer. These names will display on the Clarity
user interface and are useful for identification purposes. By default the printers are named Printer 1 - Printer 4 .
5. Choose an option from the Master/Slave Level dropdown selector.
l Group Job Select
Allows connection of up to four standard printers (with LCDs), with Job selection available only from the printer
configured as the Leader. This mode greatly reduces potential Operator errors when inputting variable data. All
other functions are performed at each local printer's LCD panel.
l Group Control
308
Rev AD

Leader- Follower Applications
Allows connection of one standard (with LCD) acting as the Leader, to up to three Follower (without LCD) print -
ers. All Job selection, Setup, Diagnostics and Error reporting are performed at the Leader printer only.
6. When ready to program the Leader printer, click OK. This is not available until at least one Follower is enabled and a level
has been selected.
If the printer was not previously configured as a Leader printer, it will need to be re- powered to accept the new con -
figuration.
7. Leave the Leader printer alone for around 30 seconds,
l The Leader printer should raise one fault for each follower printer, indicating that the follower printers are absent.
l If this does not happen, recheck the Clarity revision number installed in the printer and upgrade the printer if neces -
sary.
Connecting Printers
After wiring and configuring the printers the printers may now be connected together. After a few seconds the faults and/or warn -
ings present on the Leader and Follower printers should become clear- able, indicating successful connections.
l If any faults/warnings do not become clear- able, first try re- powering the Follower printers connected.
l If the problem persists, repeat the configuration steps for Follower and Leader printers to ensure that the Leader is con -
figured with the correct number of Followers and that no two Followers have been configured with the same number.
l If operating under Group Control mode, the faults/warnings present on the Follower printers will appear on the Leader
printer and can be cleared from there.
Troubleshooting
There’s no need to get upset right away, as the fix to your problem might be as simple as rebooting your equipment. Restarting
fixes many of issues, so make sure it’s your first response to network issues, too.
1. Power OFF your printers as well as your Connection box.
2. Wait 60 seconds (to clear the modem and router caches) then power everything ON.
Turning everything off and back on first ensures that it isn’t a temporary problem. It’s better to reboot now than to waste
30 minutes continuing on when you don’t need to.
3. If problems persist, check all physical connections before diving into settings and complex tests.
3.1. Check that the switch is plugged in.
3.2. Check cable type to ensure it is a Straight cable.
4. Once you’ve verified a proper connection, check your if lights on your switch flashing green as normal. If no lights come on
after the reboot, test to see if the device is faulty.
309
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 9- 5: 5 Port Switch
l Check for 24 Vdc at TB2.
l Check the power LED on the switch.
l Check Ground
5. On each printer, ensure that you have valid TCP/IP settings selected.
5.1. Press Tools | Diagnostics | Control | TCP/IP .
5.2. Verify the IP addresses are unique and on the same subnet . Typically the Leader is 10.0.0.128 and the followers are
10.0.0.129, 10.0.0.130, and 10.10.10.131.
Disabling Leader- Follower
If one or printers must be removed from the Leader- Follower group or must be replaced, you must properly reconfigure the con -
trollers as you remove or replace printers.
Disabling a Follower Printer
If a Follower printer is to be temporarily removed from a Leader- Follower group, you can unassign it from the Leader. If a printer
is to be permanently removed from a Leader- Follower group, follow these instructions:
1. Connect Clarity Configuration Manager to the Follower printer and wait for the coder icon to turn green.
2. Right click New Coder .
3. Click Disable Master/Slave Mode in the context menu.
4. Click Yes when prompted to confirm.
The Follower printer will leave the Leader- Follower group and begin to function as a standalone printer. You should see
the JOB button becomes available again.
310
Rev AD

Leader- Follower Applications
No Jobs in the Follower Printer Database
The Follower’s job database may be empty or contain only one job; this is because the Leader- Follower
group’s database is held by the Leader. You will need to re- download any jobs that you intend to print.
5. When disabling Follower printers the printhead mapping could become incorrect. If printhead 1 is set to 1 the image to the
left will appear. If printhead 1 is set to 2,3 or 4 the image to the right will appear. A stand alone print shall be set to print -
head 1 in order to print an image.
F IGURE 9- 6: Printhead Mapping left correct, right incorrect.
6. If you will not replace the Follower, reconfigure the Leader to only connect to the remaining Followers.
- OR-
If you are intending to replace the Follower:
6.1. Configure the new Follower.
6.2. If using basic configuration, ensure that the old Follower has been disconnected from the network and configure the
new Follower with the same Follower number as the Follower you removed; the Leader will recognize the new Follower
automatically.
6.3. If using advanced configuration (e.g. on a network) you will need to configure the new Follower with a unique IP
address, then reconfigure the Leader to connect to the new Follower’s IP address instead of the old Follower’s IP
address.
Disabling the Leader Printer
Connect to the Leader printer and select Disable Master/Slave Mode as described above. You must now either:
l Ensure that the old Leader printer has been removed from the network.
l Replace the Leader printer with a new printer given an identical configuration to the one that was removed. If using
advanced configuration, replace the Leader printer with a new printer given an identical configuration to the one that was
removed except that its IP address must be unique.
311
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
l Disable Master/Slave Mode mode for one Follower printer, then configure it to be the new Leader, connecting to the remain -
ing Followers.
l Disable Master/Slave Mode for all Followers and use them as stand- alone printers.
312
Rev AD

Communications
Overview
The 6530 printer offers multiple options for manipulating the content of jobs and the functions of the printer other than directly
working on the Clarity Controller .
Text Communications
The Text Communications (Comms) system can be used to communicate with a Clarity printer over RS232 connections and (in
software releases from June 2006) TCP/IP connections. The protocol is Leader- Follower with the host machine considered as the
master and responsible for initiating any communications.
Some legacy documents still refer to this text communications protocol as the Zipher Text Communications Protocol, however it
is no longer generally identified as such.
See "Text Communications" on page 325 for details.
Database Manager
Clarisoft has the ability to link to external data sources to include dynamic data in messages at the time the job is downloaded to
the printer. By default, Clarisoft uses its own database called "CLARiSOFT Database’" but can also be configured to use an
external database if so desired.
CLARiSOFT Database Manager enables the user to create new and manage existing CLARiSOFT databases such as adding, delet -
ing, or modifying records.
See "Database Manager" on page 343 for more details on Database Manager.
Clarisoft Print Manager
Clarisoft Print Manager is a standalone application that enables an operator to select an existing image file and download it to a
coder without the ability to change the message structure / content. This avoids the risk of accidental changes to the images or
templates in Clarisoft .
The Clarisoft Print Manager supports user- entered data and database fields if the job includes them.
See "Clarisoft Print Manager" on page 335 for more details on Clarisoft Print Manager .
313
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Clarity Data File
The Clarisoft Data File is a simple text file that allows you to provide a list of values that will be printed in sequence in a des -
ignated job. As the printer prints onto each product it prints the image with the specified field printing the next row in the data
file.
Use this option when you need to print a predetermined list of unique values to a text field or barcode and you can load these val -
ues to the controller before beginning your prints.
See "Clarity Data File" on page 319 for details.
314
Rev AD

Communications Overview
Web Server & Web Interface
The Clarity Controller can run a webserver that, when activated and connected to a network, allows a user to connect to the con -
troller from a network PC/Laptop using a web browser. The wesberver is deactivated by default. When connected, the user views
in their web browser the same interface shown on the physical Clarity Controller .
Configuring Web Server
1. Connect to printer using Clarity Configuration Manager.
2. Upload settings.
3. Expand the User Interfaces Folder.
4. Expand the Web Server Folder.
5. Change the Enabled setting to Yes.
6. Click Apply.
7. Download changes to the printer.
F IGURE 10- 1: Web Server Enabled

Connect to the Web Server that you started on the printer.
Find the Printer IP Address
To ensure you enter the correct IP address into your web browser, verify the printer IP address .
1. Press Tools | Diagnostics | Control | Communications | TCP/IP .

2. Open your web browser.
3. Enter the printer's IP address 10.0.0.150.
F IGURE 10- 3: Printer IP Address

This feature allows the printer to be controlled via a PC/Packaging machine operating a web browser.
Troubleshooting

l Make the printer IP address a trusted site.
l Check the controller Ethernet LEDs.
l Check the PC IP address.
l Check the printer's IP address.
l Ensure Web Server is enabled in Clarity Configuration Manager .
l Check condition of cable.
317
Rev AD

Clarity Data File
Clarity Data File Support allows the printer to print a sequence of images wherein part or all of the data to be printed for each
print is sourced from a text file. This allows unique traceability information to be printed at high throughput.
Use the Clarity Datafile when the customer wants the information deleted after printing, such as for serialization, but they do not
want to network to the printer (USB only).
l The CLARiTY Data file is a text file wherein each line of the text file represents the data for one print. Empty lines in the text
file will be skipped.
l The CLARiTY Data file is installed into printer using a USB memory drive. The file must be placed in the root of the USB drive
and named CLARiTY Data File.txt .
l Only one Data File is kept in memory at a time.
l When the USB device is inserted, the controller reads the Data File and prompts the user with an option to install the
new data file.
l Installing the new data file will cause entries in the old file to be lost.
l If the operator opts to install the new data file, it will be copied off the USB device onto the printer’s internal storage, the file
will be deleted from the USB device.
l The prompt when a USB key is inserted may be disabled via CLARiTY Configuration Manager. A new parameter has been
added to the folder System / USB called PromptOnCLARiTYDataFileDetect which by default is set to "1:Yes" to enable the
prompt. Setting this to "0:No" will disable the prompt and prevent data files being installed.
Job File Requirements
To utilize the data in the file in an image, the CIFF file must be be designed in CLARiSOFT with the proper fields and attributes.
l The CIFF must contain a Global Numeric counter field that uses Global Counter 14 .
l All text fields that receive information from the Data File must use the CLARiTY.Encryption.DataFile encryption component
in Text Field Properties (General Tab).
l The counter should have a sufficiently high End Value to print all lines in the text file.
l The Start Value must be set to Last Value .
l Each time the count value increases, the printer will read the next line of the file.
l The contents of the encrypted counter can be merged into other fields such as barcodes.
319
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Data File Specifications
l By default the encoding of the data file is expected to be plain ASCII text. The printer also supports Unicode and UTF- 8
encoding in the Data File.
l To use a Data file with different character encoding, go to Imaging | CLARiTY Data File | CharacterSet and choose the
desired encoding option.
l The Data File MUST be called CLARiTY Data File.txt – no other file names will be recognized by the coder when the USB key
is plugged in.
l The file MUST only be put into the root directory of the USB key as shown in Figure 11- 1 below .
F IGURE 11- 1: Text Document called CLARiTY Data File
l The data file should only contain the data, with one record per row. Blank lines will be ignored. There should be no header
row or end row, just the data.
l The file can be as long as required, however the system loads 128 rows at a time into the image buffer.
F IGURE 11- 2: Data in the text document
320
Rev AD

Clarity Data File
Creating Fields That Use the Data File
1. Open the job you want to edit or start Clarisoft and create a new file.
2. Select a counter field in Counter Properties and change it to a Global Counter number 14 as Figure 11- 4 on the next page .
3. The Restart on Rollover should be selected.
4. The maximum number of digits must be equal to the length of the data field that is being inserted.
5. The Last Value property should be selected.
F IGURE 11- 3: Counter Property Window
6. In the General tab, drop down the Encryption box and choose CLARiTY.Encryption.Datafile.1 as seen below.

Loading a Data File to the Clarity
Controller

Clarity Data File

4. Press Yes to load the data file into the coder. IT WILL THEN BE ERASED FROM THE USB KEY. This is to ensure that the
same data file cannot be loaded into either the same or another coder.
5. Make the active job the one that contains the Global Counter 14 field. If you have not created this job yet, you must create
it and load it to the printer to use the data file.
l The job will access the Data File to populate the Global Counter 14 field for each print.
l The coder will track the current position in the Data File.
l The position is held on de- selection and re- selection. Only printing advances the current position in the Data File.

F IGURE 12- 2: Select Job Command code sample.
Table 12- 1: Text Commands
Command Code Command Name
SEL Job Select (data inserted into consecutive fields)
SLA Job Select (data inserted into named fields)
JDU Job Data Update (data inserted into consecutive fields)
JDA Job Data Update (data inserted into named fields)
PRN Print
SST Set printer state
GST Get printer state

Table 12- 2: Text Commands - Version 2 or Greater
Command Code Command Name
SLI Job Select with Allocation (data inserted into named fields)
JDI Job Data Update with Allocation (data inserted into named fields)
GPC Get Counts
SPC Set Counts
The text communications system works best as a request/response protocol, where the device connected to the CLARiTY unit
sends a command to the unit, and then waits for a response before sending another command. In some cases it is possible to
send a command before the previous command has completed, but in most cases this will result in the second command failing.
To ensure that the commands you need are available and that the correct protocol mode is in use enter the Tools screen, the
press the Diagnostics button followed by Control, Communications and finally select the Communication port that is set to use
Text Communications.
The Text Comms Protocol item will now display the mode in use and installed version of the Text Comms Protocol. For example
Zipher ASCII Comms, the version 1 ASCII protocol or Zipher UNICODE Comms v2, the version 2 UNICODE protocol. To determ -
ine which commands are available in the installed version of the protocol see the table below.
To switch between ASCII and UNICODE modes use CLARiTY Configuration Manager to set System/TextCommsParameterFile to
the file name for the protocol definition file, either ZipherASCIICommsProtocol.xml or ZipherUNICODECommsProtocol.xml.
327
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Communication Sessions
A communications session comprises a set of commands or requests and responses. The carriage return character (ANSI char -
acter code 13 decimal / 0x0D hexadecimal) terminates each command or request and also has the function of resetting the
parser. This means that the carriage return character will stop parsing the current data even if the data has partially matched a
command or request. The Carriage Return- Line Feed sequence ( 0x0D 0x0A ) is also accepted as a terminator; the line feed char -
acter will simply be ignored.
Henceforth in this document, the carriage return character will be written <CR>
Most commands return either the default success response or the default failure response depending on whether or not the com -
mand was successfully executed.
The default success response is:
ACK<CR>
The default failure response is:
ERR<CR>
The examples given below use the character » to indicate data sent to the printer, and « to indicate data being received from the
printer.
For example:
»CAF<CR>
«ERR<CR> means “the string ‘CAF<CR>’ was sent to the printer, which responded with the string ‘ERR<CR>’”.
Because the carriage return character resets the parser, it is recommended to send the carriage return character before each com -
munications session in order to clear any unwanted data which may be in the parser’s buffer.
This example sends the parser reset character before sending two commands to the printer:
»<CR>
»JDU|data|<CR>
«ACK<CR>
»PRN<CR>
«ACK<CR>
Finding the Printer IP Address
To verify the printers IP address go to Tools | Diagnostics | Control | Communications | TCP/IP .
328
Rev AD

Text Communications
Commands
SEL and SLA commands cause the selection of a job on the printer. Values for variable fields may also be specified.
Syntax
SEL|<jobname>|<data>|…|<CR>
F IGURE 12- 3: SEL code sample.
SLA|<jobname>|<fieldname>=<data>|…|<CR>
SLI|<jobname>|<allocation>|<fieldname>=<data>|…|<CR>
F IGURE 12- 4: SLI code sample.
329
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Return Values
On success, SEL and SLA return the default success response, SLI returns an ID for the item placed in the job queue by this com -
mand. On failure, the default failure response is returned. If the command succeeds, the response is sent immediately.
Details
The difference between SEL and SLA lies in the way values for variable fields are matched to field names. SEL assigns consecutive
values to fields named “VarField00”, “VarField01”, “VarField02” etc; SLA allows the field name to be specified in the command,
useful if the job has fields which are not named “VarField00” etc. or if only a few fields need to be updated and using SEL would
require the inclusion of values for fields that do not need updating. If the SEL command is to be used, care should be taken when
designing the job that the fields to be updated have the correct names. SLI is similar to SLA except for the additional allocation
parameter and the return ID value.
The Job Data Update Commands (JDU and JDA/JDI)
These commands cause the variable fields on the currently selected job to be updated.
Syntax
JDU|<data>|…|<CR>
JDA|<fieldname>=<data>|…|<CR>
JDI|<allocation>|<fieldname>=<data>|…|<CR>
Return Values
On success JDU and JDA return the default success response JDI returns a queue item identifier for the job update item gen -
erated by the command. On failure the default failure response is returned. If the command succeeds, the response is sent imme -
diately.
Details
The commands JDU, JDA and JDI work in the same way as SEL, SLA and SLI respectively, except that they do not select a new
job, but update variable fields and allocations in the currently selected job.
The most likely reason for a failure on this command is trying to update fields when no job is selected. Specifying an invalid field
name for a variable field is not considered an error; the value will simply be ignored.
JD EXAMPLES
»JDU|First Field|Second Field|<CR>
«ACK<CR>
Updates variable fields on the current job; sets the field “VarField00” to the value “First Field” and the field “VarField01” to the
value “Second Field”.
»JDA|Line 1=First Line|Line 4=Fourth Line|<CR>
«ACK<CR>
Updates variable fields on the current job; sets the field “Line 1” to the value “First Line” and the field “Line 4” to the value “Fourth
Line”.
»JDI|400|Line 1=First Line|Line 4=Fourth Line|<CR>
«ACK<CR>
Updates variable fields on the current job; sets the field “Line 1” to the value “First Line” and the field “Line 4” to the value “Fourth
Line”. The allocation for the job is reset to 400.
330
Rev AD

Text Communications
The Print Command (PRN)
This command causes the currently selected job to be printed once.
Syntax
PRN<CR>
F IGURE 12- 5: PRN code sample.
Return Values
On success, returns the default success response. On failure, returns the default failure response. If the command succeeds, the
response is sent after the job has been printed.
Details
The PRN command attempts to print the current job.
Examples
»PRN<CR>
«ACK<CR>
The current job was successfully printed.
The Set State Command (SST)
This command attempts to set the overall state of the printer.
Syntax
SST|<desiredstate>|<CR>
F IGURE 12- 6: SST code sample.
Return Values
On success, returns the default success response. On failure, returns the default failure response. If the command succeeds, the
response is sent after the state transition has taken place.
Details
The SST command attempts to set the overall state. Valid values for desired state are:
l 0 Shut down
l 1 Starting up
l 2 Shutting down
331
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
l 3 Running
l 4 Offline
The most likely reason for a failure on this command is trying to perform a state transition which is not supported. Please refer to
Appendix 1 for the state transition diagram showing the permitted state transitions.
Examples
»SST|1|<CR>
«ACK<CR>
Starts up the printer
»SST|0|<CR>
«ERR<CR>
An attempt to shut down the printer. The error is caused because it is not possible to enter state 0 directly, the printer must be
moved into state 2 to shut down the printer.
The Get State Request (GST)
This request retrieves various state values from the printer.
Syntax
GST<CR>
F IGURE 12- 7: GST code sample.
Return Values
STS|<overallstate>|<errorstate>|<currentjob>|<batchcount>|<totalcount>|<CR>
Returns immediately.
Details
The GST request retrieves the following values:
l Overallstate
l The overall state of the printer, as described for the SST command.
l Errorstate
The error condition of the printer. This will be one of the following:
l 0 No errors
l 1 Warnings present
l 2 Faults present
Currentjob
332
Rev AD

Text Communications
The job selected in the printer. This will be empty if no job is selected.
Batchcount
The printer’s batch count.
Totalcount
The printer’s total count.
Examples
»GST<CR>
«STS|3|0|Default 4 Line Text|4345|8253|<CR>
The printer is running, there are no errors present, the currently selected job is “Default 4 Line Text”, the batch count is 4345 and
the total count is 8253.
»GST<CR>
«STS|4|2||0|8253|<CR>
The printer is offline with a fault, there is no job selected, the batch count is 0 and the total count is 8253.
The Clear All Faults Command (CAF)
This command attempts to clear all fault conditions present in the printer.
Syntax
CAF<CR>
F IGURE 12- 8: CAF code sample.
Return Values
On success, returns the default success response. On failure, returns the default failure response. If the command succeeds, the
response is sent after all faults have been cleared.
Details
The SST command attempts to clear all faults present in the printer. Each fault will only be cleared if it is user- clearable (i.e. if it
could be cleared using the user interface). The most likely reason for this command failing is the presence of a non- user- clearable
fault. If there are no faults present, the success response is immediately returned.
Examples
»CAF<CR>
«ACK<CR>
All faults successfully cleared
The Get Counts Command (GPC)
This command gets a snapshot of various performance counters from the printer
333
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Syntax
GPC<CR>
F IGURE 12- 9: GPC code sample.
Return Values
PCS|<success prints>|<fail prints>|<missed prints>|<remaining prints>|<CR>
Returns immediately.
Details
The GPC request retrieves the following values:
l Success prints
l The number of successful prints since start- up
l Fail prints
l The number of failed prints since start- up
l Missed prints
l The number of missed print signals since start- up
l Remaining prints
l The number of prints left in the current allocation.

Database Manager
The Clarisoft Database Manager provides an easy to use way of creating and maintaining the information used to code your
products.
CLARiCOM software provides easy ways to generate image design templates. Database Manager allows you to set up product
specific information that is inserted into the required template.
A product database stores information about each product you produce. At minimum the product database must include for
each of your products the reference code by which you wish to refer to that product and the Clarisoft image design (CIFF) that
you will print on it. For example, you produce products for major supermarkets and need to have different information printed
depending upon which supermarket a given product is designed for.
l In addition to the reference code and the CIFF, there will usually be other information that needs to be stored against each
product. The image designs you create in Clarisoft can reference any or all of this information as necessary.
l The Database Manager provides template databases designed for primary and secondary product coding to help you build
a database that matches your exact requirements.
l You can set up the Clarisoft Database in a number of ways depending upon how product selection is to be performed and
depending upon what data you have.
Database Records
Your product data is stored in rows (or records) and each record has a number of columns (or fields). The fields have a type, e.g.
text, barcodes, date offsets, etc. You can alter the configuration of the table in Database Manager’s properties dialog.
To identify your products you must provide a Unique Job Code that must be different for each product in the database. You may
have an internal product code that you can use for this purpose, if not you must devise one.
F IGURE 13- 1: The sample Clarisoft database as viewed in the Clarisoft Database Manager .
343
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Tiered Job Selection
Clarinet and Clarisoft can select products using tiered job selection . With Tiered Job Selection, up to three fields (JobCode1,
JobCode2, and JobCode3) provide options for selecting a product.
For example, you might produce for a number of supermarkets products in a variety of sizes. The jobcode fields in the Sample
database are setup in this way.
l JobCode1 references the Customer column in the database. This column contains a list of customers, the super markets.
l JobCode2 references the Product column in the database.
l JobCode3 references the Size column in the database.
Table 13- 1: Tiered job selection example
Typical Sample
Tier Internal Field Name Sample Database Name
Data
1 JobCode1 Customer Hyper Market
2 JobCode2 Product Frozen Pork Chops
3 JobCode3 Size 700g
When the operator selects the job:
1. The operator selects a Customer from the list of available values.
2. The operator selects a Product from the list of available values.
3. The operator selects a Size rom the list of available values.
If for any of the JobCode fields there is only one row in the referenced column, that entry is selected automatically and the selec -
tion process moves on to the next tier.
The only other required field is the Template Reference, or CIFF Reference, Field. This field is used to tell Clarisoft or Clarinet
which Image design template is used to print this product’s information.
If you are coding both Primary and Secondary products you may find it helpful to have two CIFF Reference fields - Clarisoft
Database Manager provides an Auxiliary CIFF Reference field. Enable this in File- Properties. By using two CIFF Reference you can
use one for Primary Products and one for Secondary products.
Running Database Manager
Database Manager installs with Clarisoft . The first time you run Clarisoft Database Manager it will load all default settings for
you.
The next time you run the Database Manager the program will open the last database you used and will restore the workspace to
the way you previously had it.

base and configuration changes.

shows that the length is wrong or the check digit on not value for the previous digits entered.
l Primary EAN barcodes must be 8 or 13 digits in Length.
l Secondary EAN product IDs (GTINs) must be 14 digits long. Primary UPC codes must be 12 digits long.

field's contents. See "Editing Memo Fields" on page 351 .

6530 Student Training Guide
Using Find
1. Select Edit | Find in the menu or type Ctrl + F. The find menu will be displayed:
2. Select from the dropdown selector the field (e.g. column) you wish to search in.
3. Enter into the Search for field the information for which you want to search.
4. Select the radio button for the Type of Search you want to execute.
l Starts With
Searches the beginning of the selected field for the string you entered. Use if you are only certain of some char -
acters at the beginning of this field in your target records.
l Contains
Searches everywhere inside the selected field for the string you entered, regardless of any characters that may
come before or after it where it occurs. Use to find all fields that contain the search string anywhere inside them.
l Exact
Searches the selected field for the exact string you entered. Use if you know the entire string (e.g. Primary EAN
barcode).
5. Click Find . The first matching record found is highlighted in the grid view.
6. Click Find Next to move to the next record found.

include the columns that fit within the printable area on the page.
3. Remove any unwanted fields by selected them and then clicking the single left arrow.

Theory of Operation
We will examine how the technologies in the Dataflex printer family work and come together to apply print at speed using
Thermal Transfer Overprinting.
Thermal Transfer Printing
The 6530 has four stepper motors and two encoders:
l Stepper Motors
l Carriage stepper motor drives the head in a linear direction.
l Printhead stepper motor drives the head in/out.
l Two ribbon stepper motors drive the ribbon and maintain ribbon tension.
l Infra- red sensors measure spool diameter and eccentricity .
l Encoders
l The encoder on the ribbon stepper motor assist iAssure and helps with ribbon tension.
l The encoder on the printhead stepper motor assist in supplying the correct printhead force when the printhead drives
in/out.
The Solid State Ribbon Drive ensures constant ribbon tension at all times. Ribbon drive consists of two perfectly synchronized
motors, controlled electronically. Ribbon tension remains constant as the ribbon moves from the supply reel to the take- up reel.
Benefits of this type of system are:
l Clutchless ribbon drive reduces downtime.
l No setup, maintenance, or wear parts are required.
l No mechanical adjustments required to maintain ribbon tension.
l Downtime associated with ribbon breaks, false ribbon breaks, overprinting, and poor print quality is reduced.
Printhead Drive
The printhead is driven by the Printhead Stepper Motor. Stepper motors are DC motors that move in discrete steps. They have mul -
tiple coils that are organized in groups called "phases". By energizing each phase in sequence, the motor will rotate, one step at a
time. With computer controlled stepping you can achieve very precise positioning and/or speed control.
The Linear Slide Stepper Motor has a holding current in continuous mode. Set this in Tools | Setup | Printhead | Print Position to
ensure the printhead is at the apex of the roller.
In intermittent mode, the head will move across the substrate. The starting point of the print is based on the horizontal regis -
tration. This parameter can be found under Tools | Setup | Printhead | Horizontal Registration .
383
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Previous models of Dataflexs used a micro switch to indicate the park position.
On the 6530 a magnet parks the head and the magnet sensor is not hand- specific. This magnet sensor is found on the camera
emitter PCB. Because of this a left handed unit will park on the right side of the printer, unlike previous models in which a left
handed printer would park on the left side.
Just as in previous models the purpose of the fixed peel roller is to flatten the ribbon to reduce creases.
Intermittent Printing Summary
1. The Carriage Stepper Motor moves the printhead assembly into its programed position, driving both belts (the Printhead
Stepper Motor is not driving the head at this time).
2. When in position, the Printhead Stepper Motor drives the printhead belt.
3. The Printhead Belt moves the head to the printing position.
4. The Printhead Encoder records the head position.
5. The Carriage Stepper Motor is at the start of the printing position (based on horizontal print position).
6. The Printhead Stepper Motor moves to the out position.
7. The Printhead Encoder maintains printhead force.
8. The Carriage Stepper Motor moves the printhead in a linear motion to transfer the image.
9. End of cycle (Intermittent)
9.1. The Printhead Stepper Motor moves head to the In position.
9.2. The Carriage Stepper Motor pulls the printhead assembly back to the print position (the head belt does nothing at
this time).
Continuous Printing Summary
1. The Carriage Stepper Motor moves the printhead assembly into its programed position (print position), driving both belts
(the Printhead Stepper Motor is not driving the head at this time).
2. When in position, the Printhead Stepper Motor drives the printhead belt.
3. The Printhead Belt moves the head to the printing position.
4. The Printhead Encoder records the head position.
5. The Carriage Motor will have a holding voltage applied.
6. The Printhead Stepper Motor moves the printhead to the out position.
7. The Printhead Encoder maintains the correct printhead force.
8. End of cycle (Continuous)
8.1. The Carriage Holding Voltage remains applied (until the unit goes off line).
8.2. The Printhead Stepper Motor moves head to the In position.
Ribbon Calibration
Ribbon Calibration takes place every time a cassette is loaded or the printer is powered up. Upon completion, the calibration
provides us with a ribbon diameter reading within 0.1 – 0.2 mm, thus allowing extremely accurate ribbon feed accuracy and
repeatability.
384
Rev AD

Theory of Operation
F IGURE 15- 1: Emitter
1. The stepper motor turns the Take- up Mandrel allowing the mandrel to engage into the motor drive coupling.
2. The Printer PCB detects the engagement of the take- up mandrel via the change in tension at the Supply Mandrel as the
waste mandrel is driven by the waste motor.
3. The Printhead unparks itself and traverses once along the linear slide carriage.
4. Initial optical calibration commences.
l The 28 static infrared emitters are sensed by the static infrared detector sensor (mounted to the top of the printer)
providing an initial measurement of the ribbon diameters.
l The emitter LEDs fire one at a time leaving a shadow of both ribbon diameters.
5. The continuous feed of ribbon engages the Supply Mandrel into its motor drive coupling.
6. With both spools engaged and the head carriage parked, the Take- up Mandrel feeds a precise amount of ribbon while
detecting the tension at the supply mandrel. This allows an accurate measurement of the diameter ratio between the two
mandrels.
7. Further feeding and rewinding ensures that the ribbon tension ends up at the desired nominal tension.
8. The ribbon is fed and rewound with the head parked.
9. The recorded size of the ribbon is now accessible in the Clarity interface by pressing Tools | Diagnostics | Consumables .
F IGURE 15- 2: The Ribbon diameters shown in the Consumables menu.
385
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
6530 Intermittent & Continuous
6530 features intermittent and continuous operation in the same TTO.
l No risk of printer becoming obsolete when upgrading intermittent form/fill/seal machines to continuous
form/fill/seal or vise verse.
l TTO can be moved from one line to another regardless of operation mode.
l Compatible with newer form/fill/seal machines offering both intermittent and continuous operation.
Intermittent Printing
In Intermittent mode, the film must come to a complete stop before print occurs.
F IGURE 15- 3: Diagram: Intermittent Printing
Print Action Theory of Operation
1. Receive Print Signal.
2. Allow Print Delay to elapse (~20msecs).
3. Activate Head Down and allow head down timer to elapse.
4. Print Busy Output engaged.
5. Printhead begins to sweep, first covering the Start Border Distance .
6. The head continues to sweep, heating individual print elements to print the image.
7. Head return activated.
8. Print Busy Output disengaged.
9. Carriage returns and new ribbon is advanced.
Ribbon Advance Speed – variable between 300 and 500 mm/sec. The system will always try to feed the ribbon as fast as
it is able.
386
Rev AD

Theory of Operation
10 mm Minimum Feed
When printing a small image length, for example 5mm, then the ribbon will feed 15mm. The system needs
10mm of feed at full ribbon advance speed, which is the minimum required for tension monitoring, and the
back feed 10mm.
Checking Print Speeds
Maximum and Minimum Print speeds achieved during a print cycle can be seen in the Timings Screen.
Tools | Diagnostics | Print head | Timings
Set the Print Delay
Intermittent Mode Printers ONLY!
Print Delay is the time that the printer waits between receiving a print signal and starting a print. It can be increased to ensure
that the target material has come to a complete stop before printing starts. If printing occurs while the target material is still mov -
ing, then the resulting image may appear to be stretched or squashed. To enable maximum throughput, the Print Delay should
be set to the shortest time that produces consistent prints.
1. From the HOME screen, press Tools | Setup | Printhead .
2. Press the PRINT DELAY parameter.
3. Use the keyboard to enter a new value.
Set the Print Speed
Intermittent Mode Printers ONLY!
The print speed set at the printer causes the code to adhere more readily to some types of target materials than to others. You can
increase the print speed to improve print quality.
1. From the HOME page, press Tools | Setup | Printhead .
2. Press the PRINT SPEED parameter.
3. Use the keyboard to enter a new value.
Continuous Printing
In Continuous printing mode, the substrate is moving during the print cycle.
387
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 15- 4: Diagram: Film movement in continuous printing.
The packaging film is constantly moving during the wrapping and sealing cycle, so a continuous bracket assembly with print
roller and encoder is required.
Print Action Theory of Operation
The print roller allows the printhead of the 6530 to remain in a fixed position and pivot (move in and out) against the ribbon and
moving film, printing the full image required. To achieve good print quality, the printhead of the 6530 is set via the “Print Pos -
ition” parameter to a fixed point against the print roller radius.
1. Print Signal is received.
2. Registration distance starts to elapse.
3. By means of a complicated algorithm, the ribbon accelerates (using previously used ribbon).
4. Head Down is activated, ensuring that the head is in contact with a fresh piece of ribbon and that the head is traveling at
the same speed as the substrate.
5. Head Return is activated and ribbon decelerates to rest very quickly.
Early Print Signals
The print signal for the next print can be accepted and registration distance can be elapsing before completion
of the previous print, maximizing throughput.
388
Rev AD

Theory of Operation
Adjusting Print Position (Continuous)
Adjusting Print Position will make the printing corner of the head press against different points on the roller. This alters the angle
at which the printhead presses against the target material. The angle affects the print quality. If the angle is not suitable, then the
resulting print may appear faded. To find a suitable Print Position:
1. From the HOME page, press Tools | Setup | Printhead .
2. Press the PRINT POSITION parameter.
3. Use the on- screen keyboard to enter a new value.
4. Try changing the value in steps of 1mm. The print position parameter is in 0.01mm units, so a change of 100 units will
move the print head by 1mm.
5. Examine sample prints from each position until you find a range of values in which the image is of high quality.
6. Set the Print Position to be in the middle of the range of high quality prints.
Motor Calibration
Motor calibration should only be necessary you replace a board or a motor. The motor calibration is an event triggered from the
Clarity Configuration Manager Software which maps and stores the individual characteristics of the ribbon drive motors. This is
achieved with a cassette present and mandrels engaged, but without ribbon present. The sequence takes a few minutes, during
which time the motors are rotated at different speed levels which may be audibly detected by changes in pitch. The calibration
sequence includes 20 repeat cycles giving very accurate measurements.
Correct motor calibration is essential to ensure the ribbon tension system remains accurate and virtually eliminates erroneous rib -
bon breaks.
To conduct a motor calibration proceed with following steps:
1. Remove the cassette holder and remove the ribbon from the cassette.
2. Reinstall the cassette holder into the printer.
3. Ensure that your computer is connected to the Clarity Controller .
4. Open up the Clarity Configuration Manager software.
5. Right click on New Coder .
6. Select Upload from the context menu.
7. After the upload completes, click Operations in the right panel.
8. Click on PerformMotorCalibration .
9. Click Trigger in the lower left corner and click Yes to confirm when prompted.
The ribbon motors will rotate through their entire range of motion. Motors will be quite noisy during this process.
10. Allow the process to complete. This process may take 5 minutes or more.
11. After the motor calibration is complete, reload the ribbon in the cassette to resume normal operation.
389
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Tension Monitoring
Every time the ribbon is fed on a 6530, the system monitors the tension and, if necessary, makes slight adjustments to maintain
nominal tension range. This ability is enhanced by the use of an encoder. Once a reel has been operating for a while, the tension
monitoring system will only need to make adjustments approximately every tenth print or so. The most likely time that a higher
frequency of adjustments would be required would be when a new 1000 meter reel is loaded.
Three Modes of Tension Failure exist:
l Gross slack in system is an instant fail syndrome.
l Two successive readings within the course band threshold.
l Six readings outside the ideal band but within the fine band threshold.
Note:
When set to 55 mm ribbon, the nominal tension target is 2200. The maximum permissible would be approx -
imately 50% above this figure and we would always expect the minimum permissible level to be greater than
zero.
If sine wave is too high or too low the system will fail.
You can view the ribbon tension reading on the Clarity Controller under Tools | Diagnostic | Consumables .
F IGURE 15- 5: Ribbon tension reported in the Consumables menu.
390
Rev AD

Installation
TTO technology requires physical contact with target materials at high throughput and placement within other equipment to
reach that target film at the correct time. Planning and proper installation are critical to the success of TTO printers. Post install -
ation is less forgiving than in other technologies, so it is worth taking the time to get things correct the first time.
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Unless it
is necessary to run the printer, disconnect the printer from the mains electrical supply before removing the cov -
ers, or attempting any service or repair activity. Failure to follow this warning can cause death or personal
injury.
Personal Injury
Improper use of this equipment may result in personal injury.
Possible Equipment Damage
Improper use of this equipment may result in equipment damage.
Possible Equipment Damage
Power the printer OFF before connecting or disconnecting any cables or equipment to or from the printer.
DO NOT connect cables or other equipment to the printer while it is powered on.
TTO Applications
Customer applications that require TTO printers will vary and every installation is unique. It will help to familiarize yourself with
the most common machines in which our printers will be installed.
Vertical Bagging Machines
Vertical Bagging Machines operate in conjunction with multi- head weighers.
l Continuous and intermittent mode available.
l Usually used for individual bags, sachets, or packets.
l The printer is generally mounted underneath the web path.
391
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 16- 1: Illustration of a Vertical Form Fill machine.
Vertical Form, Fill and Seal (VFFS) applications use gravity to feed (drop) product into a partially formed bag which is then sealed
after filling. VFFS machines often have built in registration. The purpose of the registration unit is to allow the print position on
the packaging film to be moved into the required area.
Figure 16- 1 above shows the typical flow of the film packaging material in a VFFS application.
l Typically, the film is fed from a roll at the back of the packaging unit, up onto the top of the unit, and onto the forming col -
lar as shown in Figure 16- 1 above .
l The bag is sealed on one side to form a tube (fin seal).
l At the sealing jaws, the jaws close to seal the top of one bag and the bottom of the next.
l When the bottom of the bag is sealed the product falls in and the jaws close to seal the top of that bag and the bottom of
the next, and so on….
VFFS machines (aka: baggers ) are limited in speed based on how fast products can fall.
l The forming collar converts the film from a flat shape to a tube.
Print Quality Issues at the Collar
A print quality issue to watch for - The angle the film wraps around the collar is very sharp and can
scrape some of the image off of the printed film.
l The sealing jaws seal the top of the filled bag and the bottom of the next empty bag that is being formed.
Print Quality Issues at the Sealing Jaws
Print quality issues – what to look for - They are often heated so it is important to make sure the printed
image is not in this area of the package.
392
Rev AD

Installation
F IGURE 16- 2: Front view of a VFFS machine.
Horizontal machines
F IGURE 16- 3: Illustration of a Horizontal Form Fill machine.
l Flow wrappers are usually used with softer products or collations – cake, bakery products, multi- bags.
l Individual pockets or flight bars separate the products.
l Many flow wrappers are cantilevered in design.
l Continuous and intermittent modes available.

Actions Requiring Customer Assistance

form the following:

l Any required climbing or like to integrate 6530 equipment
Videojet personnel are not to perform any of these actions.
Environment
l Ambient Temperature
Is temperature within specifications?
l < 40 F (5 C) min = SAR Heater may be required
l >104 F (40 C) max= Cooling fan may be required
l Ambient Humidity
Extreme condensation is fairly common on refrigerated rooms
l Significant condensation may require the use of SAR Heater
l Condensation moisture the film can inhibit the TTO’s ability to transfer full image
l Dust Contamination
Extreme dust/particle contamination will require periodic cleaning
l Wash- Down / Wet Environment
l The 6530 has no IP rating.
l Bagging TTO is not recommended.
TTO in Wet Environments
How harsh an environment can a TTO printer operate in? TTO printers have a very low IP rating due to
the necessary open area where the print head comes into contact with the substrate. Therefore, areas
where wash down occurs will require the printer to be installed with an IP65 housing, as will the oper -
ator interface, due to the “D” type connections and communication ports. Dust or fluids on the sub -
strate could put the printhead at risk and cause excessive wear or thermal shock. Therefore, the cleaner
and drier the environment the better.
The enclosure is for wash down only the unit will not print with the IP65 enclosure in place.
395

Important Note
