how integration is determined to take place
Intermittent
F IGURE 16- 6: Illustration of Registration Unit.
A registration unit allows the print position on the packaging film to be moved into the required area. By adjusting the regis -
tration roller up or down, the length of the film between the supply reel and the sealing jaws of the packaging machine is made
longer or shorter. This changes the printing position on the film accordingly.
The registration unit should, ideally, be positioned between the sealing jaws of the packaging machine and the printer. This will
ensure the print position can be changed when the registration roller position is adjusted.
Registration units are used on the packaging film in intermittent applications. Occasionally, there are continuous applications
where the film is moving too quickly or too slowly for the 6530 to print. By using the registration unit, it may be possible to adjust
the film path to a faster or slower part of the packaging cycle, allowing a suitable opportunity for printing with the 6530.
In order to achieve the best quality print from a continuous application, the substrate should wrap a minimum of 90 degrees
around the print roller.
398
Rev AD

Installation
F IGURE 16- 7: Registration Roller Intermittent
Intermittent Considerations
When adopting the Modular System for intermittent packaging applications, the following information is important and will
affect the specified solution outcome.
The width of the reel carriage, and therefore the maximum permissible substrate material, is a key factor in specifying the
required bracket width. The Modular System is designed with a flat pack dimension of 1m wide (wider applications are rare but
possible by specifying separately). The common denomination to specify a bracket width is known as the A Dimension (all other
cutting lengths on a bracket width are arrived at by simple formulae derived from the A Dimension). The A Dimension is the
overall width of a fully assembled bracket, measured to the outside surface of the end plates.
Where does the bracket go? Each Packaging machine will have a different web path configuration, but all can be categorized
into three groups. Templates are available, which, when held up to the machine can mimic the intended bracket position, veri -
fying that appropriate space is available and its effect on other reel gear on the machine. The majority of VFFS Machines allow
mounting of a Modular System on the top portion of the machine or above the supply reel gear, as shown in Figure 16- 7 above .
Height clearance above the machine (due to platform where the products are to be weighed) can sometimes be limiting, depend -
ing on the type of product being packed. Where space is not available then the rear of the machine is used but the further the
coder is mounted from the sealing point of the bag means that more bags can be wasted on changeover
Positioning of guide rollers will vary from application to application, but because they are “plug and play” it is easy to adjust
these rollers on installation. The criteria for the roller positioning are that they must present the substrate in the correct plane to
the print head and that also the web should not foul on any stationary part of the bracket or machine frame, causing additional
web drag.
The Modular System, when fitted to intermittent machines, must have adequate adjustment to print at any point across the width
of the bag or at any point along the length of the longest bag. The adjustment across the width of the substrate is taken care of
by manually moving the printer across the bracket, and the adjustment up and down the bag length is achieved by implementing
a registration module. The registration module allows the stopping position of the film to change in relation to the print head and
is effected by adjustment of a moveable roller up and down a rack.
Continuous
Why do we need the registration roller in continuous mode?
399
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 16- 8: Registration Roller Continuous
The majority of continuous applications do not require a registration unit as the substrate does not stop moving, an electronic
delay can be set within the 6530, when the delay expires, the coder receives the signal to print. All continuous printers will require
an encoder to match the substrate speed to print speed.
Continuous Considerations
As with the intermittent considerations, it is important that the bracket is able to cope with the widest substrate on the applic -
ation. The A dimension should reflect this. On HFFS machines, which are generally continuous, the reel gear is quite often can -
tilevered in design, as opposed to the traditional VFFS machine which offers bracket support at both ends. This does not cause a
problem as the Modular Bracket design is very strong. However, once the reel gear width increases beyond 600mm, then it is com -
mon practice to brace the suspended end of the bracket. This is achieved either by bracing to other rollers cantilevered on the par -
ent machine or to the product transport table.
As with vertical machines it is essential that the coder is always mounted after the break dancing arm (which is normally imme -
diately after the supply reel and is intended to prevent reel overrun). On continuous wrappers, however, it is also more common to
find automatic splicing systems, for parent machines which offer a twin reel facility. It is also essential on these machines that
the coder is mounted after these assemblies.
For Registration adjustment along the pack length, this is normally affected electronically either at the coder, but more normally
by the parent machine.
Another consideration when gathering information on continuous applications is whether the machine runs plain or print
registered film. Fitting of a print registration sensor needs to be a consideration where high accuracy of print position is required
and the wrapper has auto- bag length correction.
Divert Rollers change the path of the film, allowing it to be:
l Diverted easily out of the existing film path
l Move freely through the printer
l Diverted back into the existing film path

Check Sheet
This is the installation check sheet for a basic TTO install. Your actual install may vary.
1. Meet with customer to review installation requirement and specifications.
2. Complete customer’s onsite health and safety documentation as required.
3. Customer to provide terminal pin outs for host machine inputs.
4. Unpack equipment and check against zero defect.
5. Test 6530 power up sequence.
6. Assist customer with loading Configuration Manager and purchased CLARiSOFT.
7. Check supply voltage with DVM.
8. Have customer isolate and lock out electrical of host machine.
9. Measure and assemble universal bracket based on installation specifications or SAR.
10. Mount universal bracket or SAR.
11. Mount clarity controller and bracket.
12. Install encoder.
13. Install and adjust printer to bracket.
14. Connect low profile cable and mount photocell if required.
15. Connect print trigger and fault outputs to host machine.
16. Connect power cable to electrical supply.
17. Remove lock out power.
18. Re- web host machine with film (customer responsibility)
19. Configure the printer.
20. Confirm print signals.
21. Confirm fault outputs with customer.
22. Test application.
23. Insert passwords (make note of password for customer).
24. Archive configuration manager settings.
25. Backup CLARiSOFT jobs on PC and USB (CSE and customer).
26. Cable tie wiring as necessary.
27. Clean up packing material and work area.
28. Basic training on equipment operation and maintenance.
29. Training on CLARiSOFT.
30. Technician fills out document for customer signature
31. Closed off health and safety documents.

Assembling the Bracket
When assembling the bracket system, it is important to ensure the bracket is square.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not over tighten bolts as this will result in equipment damage.
1. Place support tube blanking caps into the side plate support tubes and use a rubber mallet or soft hammer to tap the sup -
port tube blanking cap until fully seated.
F IGURE 16- 18: Support Tube Blanking Caps.
2. Repeat 8 times so that all caps are installed.
3. Assemble the printer mounting assembly.
4. Place (2) side plate support tubes into the printer mounting assembly.
F IGURE 16- 19: Side Support Tubes latched to the Printer Mounting Assembly.
5. Fit the side plate support tubes into a side plate. These side plate support tubes are mounted opposite the registration
roller holes.
l Ensure the recessed bolt holes are facing down.
l If you will use a platen roller, install it at this time.
410
Rev AD

Installation
F IGURE 16- 20: Side plate support tubes fitted into Side Plate.
6. Place the other (2) side plate support tube into the side plate.
7. Tighten the bolts that secure the side plate tubes to the side plate.
8. Place the second side plate on top. ensuring that your bracket is square.
l Ensure both registration holes are on the same side.
l Ensure the recessed bolt holes are facing up.
9. Tighten the bolts that secure the side plate tubes to the side plates.
10. If intermittent, assemble the platen pad and attach to the side plate support tube.
Your bracket should look like Figure 16- 21 below .
F IGURE 16- 21: Example: Fully assembled bracket for Intermittent.

F IGURE 16- 23: Platen Roller hole in the universal bracket.

6. Attach the platen roller assembly to the side plates.

Mounting Rollers
The bracket system uses many different types of rollers standard and food grade are the most popular types. There are pressed
and heavy duty as well.
Now that the bracket is built we can start mounting the diverting rollers. It is important that the diverting rollers have been cut to
the correct size.
F IGURE 16- 26: Diverting Roller Layout
Food grade diverting rollers are different from the conventional diverting rollers and require a mounting tool that comes in the
bracket kit.
413
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 16- 27: Diverting Roller (left) and Mounting Tool (right).
1. Place the bearing end cover cap over the side plate support tubes. If using food grade rollers follow instructions for food
grade rollers.
2. Insert the stub axle collar onto the stub axle.
3. Tighten the stub axle collar so that it is not slipping but not fully tightened. This will be done later.
4. Repeat steps 1- 3 at the other end.
5. Place the diverting roller into the bracket at the desired bolt hole.
6. Using a wrench to lock the stub axle, screw in your bolt and tighten.
F IGURE 16- 28: Tightening the Stub Axle
7. Repeat steps 5 and 6 on the other side.
8. Center your diverting roller.
9. Fully tighten you (2) stub axle collars.
Registration Assembly
To assemble the registration roller use the following steps:
1. Attach the Toothed Rails to the side plates using M5 x 12mm screws. The counter sunk holes should be on opposite side of
rails.
414
Rev AD

Installation
F IGURE 16- 29: End Plates with Toothed Rails
2. Cut the registration shaft. End- to- end dimension of the shaft including threads should be 2.5” longer than the stainless
steel support tubes on the universal bracket.
3. Thread the 10mm half nut onto the shaft. If nut is not available, skip this step and move onto step 4.
4. Thread the threaded pinion (without the set screws) onto the shaft.
5. Be sure the half nut and pinion is threaded all the way down to where the threads meet the solid end of the rod. Insert the
threaded end of the shaft through the end plate slot with the toothed rail on the inside.
6. Insert a 10mm washer on the threaded side of the shaft, moving washer toward the end plate.
7. Thread the hand- wheel knob (one with threads all the way through the handle) onto the shaft.
8. Thread the 10mm full nut onto the shaft.
9. Thread second hand- wheel knob to end of shaft.
F IGURE 16- 30: Completed Assembly of Registration Roller and Hand Wheel Slide
10. Cut the aluminum rollers approximately 2.75” shorter than the stainless steel support tubes on the universal bracket.
11. Insert the bearing end covers on each end of the aluminum rollers.
415
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
12. Insert one aluminum roller onto non- threaded end of shaft towards the10mm half nut. If the half nut is not available,
insert a 10mm washer before inserting aluminum roller.
13. Insert shaft collar (small collar) onto non- threaded end of shaft. DO NOT SECURE THE COLLAR.
14. Insert the un- threaded pinion onto non- threaded end of shaft. DO NOT SECURE THE COLLAR SETSCREWS.
15. Insert the other 10mm washer onto non- threaded end of shaft.
16. Insert second end plate with tooth rail on the inside.
F IGURE 16- 31: Completed Assembly of Registration Roller Shaft on Non- Hand Wheel Side.
17. Insert stub axles into the end caps of second aluminum roller. Attach stub axle collar to each stub axle. Attach second alu -
minum roller to end plates using M6 x 16mm screws.
F IGURE 16- 32: Completed Registration Roller
18. Secure the Registration Assembly to the universal bracket using the connecting blocks and M6 x 20mm screws.
Registration Roller Adjustments
The Registration roller can be adjusted so that the print window on the film is under the printer in intermittent printing mode.
1. Push the registration roller all the way to one end. Make sure roller is straight.
2. Secure the shaft collar and pinion setscrews. The registration roller should travel freely and straight along the rails without
binding.
3. To lock the registration roller in place, tighten hand- wheel closest to the end plate and tighten the full 10mm nut against
the hand- wheel. Make sure the knob is threaded to a stop before securing the nut.

The following maintenance should be carried out on the 6530 by service engineer during a customer site visit.
l Clean, inspect or replace encoder O- ring

l Export iAssure failure to USB
l Export Performance | Availability to USB

l Verify printer is square to the bracket - adjust as needed
l Verify printer is parallel to the bracket and substrate - adjust as needed

l Clean camera

l Clean emitter (this is now the bank of LEDs on the CR&P board)

Basic Troubleshooting
Optical Fault
l Ribbon Break
l Broken ribbon. Reinstall the ribbon.
l Too much slack in ribbon when cassette is loaded reinstall the ribbon.
l End of reel replace ribbon reel.
l Ribbon gets wrong side of peel roller when loading cassette. Reinstall the ribbon.
l Host machine grabbing ribbon with too much force. Review application and fix.
Print quality problems:
l Check condition of head and platen.
l Check printhead force and darkness.
l Check gap from head to platen.
l Check printhead belt.
Calibration failure fault:
l Are spools secure on hubs?
l Are cassette mandrels engaging fully?
l Check condition of drive belt.
l Sensors clean and undamaged?
l Are all motors being driven?
l Is the encoder reading?
Printer malfunctioning:
l Use Diagnostics to isolate fault.
l Inputs status may be checked.
l Outputs may be toggled.
False ribbon breaks:
l Is correct ribbon width selected?
l Are ribbon cores secure on hubs?
l Is ribbon correct type?
l Is ribbon threaded correctly?
Troubleshooting using the diagnostics page.
420
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
To access the Diagnostic Pages:
1. Press on the Tools button (near the top right of the screen).
2. Press on the Diagnostics panel; the following page will be displayed.
3. Select from the three areas, the area where the problem is suspected to exist.
If it is suspected that the printer is not correctly setting one of its outputs, e.g. the fault output is not being set correctly, this can
be tested by toggling the output.
1. First, the printer must be Offline.
2. Go to the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Outputs .
3. Press on the toggle button for the appropriate output.
The displayed state of the output will change. If the physical output does not change accordingly, then there could be a problem
with the output on the printer.
The Host Packaging Machine Won’t Run:
If CLARiTY is displaying a fault, then the printer will be activating its fault output. The fault output is intended to be wired to inter -
lock and stop the host packaging machine. Clear the fault see section ‘Clear a fault or warning’ to clear the fault. This will switch
the fault output and allow the host packaging machine to run again.
The state of the output can be examined on the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead Outputs .
This diagnostic should be checked to determine what the expected output of the printer should be.
Only Half of the Image Comes Out:
Note: In software version R06, the printer will fault with a Image Outside Print Area, if the vertical moves the
image off of the ribbon.
l Check printhead mapping. The printhead 1 should be set to 1.
l Check the alignment of the printhead bracket.
l Check your vertical and horizontal registration settings.
l Check for a bent peel roller.
The Print Signal is Connected, But It Doesn’t Print:
View the diagnostics on the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Inputs . The state of the print signal input that the printer is
receiving is listed.
l Manually trigger the print sensor/signal and check whether the diagnostic page shows a change in the state of the signal. If
the diagnostics do not show a change, then it is likely that the print signal is not being generated correctly.
l Check all of the print signal/sensors connections. If the diagnostic page still does not show the expected read out, then
change the print signal/sensor.
The Print Appears Smeared:
l If the printer is working intermittently (the target material is stationary while printing is taking place), then the material may
not be coming to a complete rest before the print begins. Alter the Print Delay setting to correct the problem (see CLARITY
Configuration Manager: Set the Print Delay).
l Bent peel roller.
421
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Ribbon Tracking
l Starting the ribbon webbing with a crease, a gap or ribbon bunched up near the washer.
l Not aligning the printer with the printer alignment tool.
l Damaged cassette (bent rollers, washers not level or damaged mandrels).
l Bracket not aligned (printhead not coming down on the platen evenly, poor install or damaged bracket).
l Too much vertical registration, job not centered on the ribbon, catching the ribbon on one side.
l Substrate walking to one side.
l Too much darkness or print force, ribbon could be sticking to substrate.
l Hub not seated fully on the washer.
l Hub not seated fully on the washer.
l Missing black and silver disc.
l iAssure lightbox out off alignment.
l Printhead not engaging with magnets correctly when in OFFLINE state. If the head is not engaging correctly with the mag -
nets, this will leave a incorrect gap between the Printhead and peel roller- this may cause the ribbon to track upon cal -
ibration.
l Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | iAssure | Manufactures Password | Status | Capture Ribbon Positions. Check for tracking on
the graph.
RIBBON WRINKLING
Ribbon wrinkling condition is more commonly seen on wider ribbon thermal transfer applications, 107mm units usually under cer -
tain application conditions.
It has been noted that certain image types such as large solid areas/ barcodes and dense fine print can be more conducive to the
formation of wrinkles.
Sometimes, eliminating ribbon wrinkles can be very difficult because they can be induced by one or a combination of several
factors such as:
l Printer to bracket platen alignment.
l Printhead gap.
l Printhead Pivot assembly condition and/or adjustment.
l Worn/Defective Rubber pad.
l Wrong rubber pad texture/hardness.
l Worn/Defective cassette.
l Worn/Dirty Peel Roller or Bent Peel Roller Arm.
l Defective ribbon, Ribbon type/grade or Incorrect ribbon width/type settings.
l Incorrect CLARiTY Configuration Parameter settings.
l Excessive Print Darkness.
l Excessive Print Force.
l Printhead Speed.
l Encoder settings/adjustment/Platen Roller (continuous operation)
422
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
l Excessive pressure on the encoder tension spring.
l Host Machine (inadequate film tension, speeds, steep accelerating/decelerating rates, etc.).
l Substrate.
NO PRINT (PH not moving) intermittent
l No Print Signal Confirm Print Signal contact is received by the printer (Tools/Diagnostics/Printhead Inputs/Input Con -
figuration/ External Sensor 1). Refer to maintenance manual for print signal wiring connections. Also, Batch Count and
Total Count not increasing are indicative of no print signal contact activity.
l Belts not moving - Toggle the head In/Out.
l Wrong setting- Set for continuous printing.
NO PRINT (PH is moving; making contact) intermittent
l Incorrect Print Timing - Confirm actual printing is not taking place when the film is moving. Film must be fully settled (dur -
ing dwell time) when printing occurs. Use Print Delay as necessary (Tools/Set Up/ Printhead).
l Image out of PH Range – Confirm Vertical & Horizontal Registration are not set to a high value. Set both values to 0 and
check for print.
l Harsh Environmental Conditions – Refrigerated rooms and/or condensation can impact print quality. Different ribbon
grade or heater use may be required.
l High Printhead Speed – Set Printhead Speed to default 200mm/s (Tools/Setup/Printhead). Lower speed further if using
Premium Ribbon (resin only, “R” ribbon).
l Incorrect Printhead Gap - Set to 3.75mm from peel roller for any standard application or 4.5mm for zipper application.
l Low Darkness - Increase Darkness setting until good print quality & adhesion are achieved
l Substrate & Ribbon Incompatibility – Ribbon may not be suitable for certain substrates. Test different ribbon grade. Sub -
strate may not be TTO compatible.
l Incorrect Ribbon Placement - Check that ribbon was placed on cassette and through the printhead/peel roller correctly.
l Defective or Non VJ Ribbon - Confirm VJ ribbon is used. Replace with new roll.
l Loose printer – Causes bracket misalignment. Confirm Printhead Mounting Plate ‘T’ screw handle and the four mounting
screws are secured and tight (if longer screws were used, they will bottom out and printer will be loose).
l Loose Print Platen – Confirm Print Platen ‘T’ screw handle is tight.
l Rubber Pad Peeled Off – Check to make sure the rubber pad is entirely adhered to the platen and no section or corner has
peeled off the platen. There should be no air bubbles. Replace rubber pad as necessary.
RIBBON BREAKS (real or false)
l Incorrect Print Timing - Confirm actual printing is not taking place when the film is moving. Film must be fully settled (dur -
ing dwell time) when printing occurs. Use Print Delay as necessary (Tools/Set Up/ Printhead).
l Incorrect Printhead Gap - Set to 3mm for any standard application or 4.5mm for zipper applications.
l Ribbon Width Selection – Set correct ribbon width and type. Selection must match actual ribbon used (Tools/Set Up/Con -
sumables).
l Excessive Pressure - Check printhead force.
423
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
l Defective Cassette - Check cassette condition; especially mandrels and corner rollers. Mandrels must rotate freely, no bind -
ing. If cassette has been dropped, replace it with a new one. If a second unit is on site, swap cassettes to confirm if original
one is damaged.
l Defective or non VJ Ribbon - Check for defective/damaged ribbon roll or non VJ ribbon. Use new VJ ribbon roll.
l Loose printer - Causes bracket misalignment. Confirm Printhead Mounting Plate ‘T’ screw handle and the four mounting
screws are secured and tight (if longer screws were used, they will bottom out and printer will be loose).
l Loose Print Platen – Confirm Print Platen ‘T’ screw handle is tight.
l Rubber Pad Peeled Off – Check to make sure the rubber pad is entirely adhered to the platen and no section or corner has
peeled off the platen. There should be no air bubbles. Replace rubber pad as necessary.
l Incorrect Ribbon Placement - Check that ribbon was placed on cassette and through the printhead/peel roller correctly.
Ribbon Tension
l Ensure the correct ribbon settings (Tools/Setup/Consumables) are configured for the ribbon that is being used.
l Ensure the printer is aligned/square with the bracket.
l Ensure proper bracket integrity (not loose, misaligned, etc.).
l Ensure printhead comes down flush and evenly across the print surface.
l If continuous application, ensure the printhead is directly over the center/crown/apex of the print roller by adjusting the
“Printhead Position”.
l If continuous application, ensure the encoder O- ring is in good condition and rides snug on the print roller or film to ensure
no slippage takes place.
l If continuous application, the “Continuous Maximum Print Speed” setting in CLARiTY Configuration Manager can be
lowered to be slightly above the actual film speed. Perform an encoder profile to see the max film speed when printing.
Then lower the “Continuous Maximum Print Speed” to be slightly above that.
l Ensure both the black and silver discs are on the cassette to prevent ribbon from telescoping off the mandrels.
l Inspect the cassette for damaged/bent/misaligned/missing components.
l Try to use the lowest possible darkness setting while still achieving desired print quality.
l Ensure the proper print force (2.0 – 3.5 kg 53mm), (2.0 – 6.0 kg 107mm), (1.0 – 2.5 kg 32mm).
l Ensure the leaf springs are in the correct orientation based on the ribbon width.
l If the issue is occurring on a minimal number of units, try performing a motor calibration when the motors have cooled for
at least 15 minutes.
l If all of the above check out fine and it is a continuous application, the “Encoder Scaling” setting in CLARiTY Configuration
Manager can be adjusted from 100 to 98. 98 is not some magic number but we have found it to be somewhat effective. Be
aware that too drastic of a change to this setting can have other adverse effects and this setting should only be adjusted as
a last resort.
Printhead Carriage Position Error
l Ensure the printhead gap does not exceed 5mm.
l Do not print on air.
l Does the carriage move with a bit of resistance, free from any obstructions, debris, or binding.
l If there is slight binding, try cleaning the linear slide by blowing it out with dry, compressed air. You can also lubricate the
slide using the VJ linear slide lubrication kit (PN: 402113).
424
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
l Inspect the peel roller shaft and peel arm assembly ensuring they are not bent as this could keep the printhead from coming
down unobstructed. Replace if bent.
l Inspect the belts for wear or physical damage, replace as necessary.
l Ensure there are no obstructions that are keeping the printhead from reaching the park position. A loose light box screw that
can keep the printhead from reaching its full parked position.
l If you hear a grinding noise, inspect the pulleys and ensure no physical damage or that they have seized. If seized, replace
the pulleys.
l If you hear a grinding noise and the pulleys are fine, inspect the printhead motor connections on the main PCB ensuring the
connectors on the main PCB are fully seated and also that the connections on the motors are plugged into the correct con -
nectors on the main PCB. The motor connections need to be connected into the connectors on the main PCB closest to the
motors.
l If all the above checks out fine, the next option is to tension the belt.
l If all of the above check out fine and re- tensioning the belt did not resolve, it could be a faulty printhead motor w/encoder
sensor.

Printer Errors
This section contains information on error messages, it is possible causes and remedies.
Cassette Open
l Cassette not fully seated, push cassette in until you hear it “click” into place.
l Cassette latch out of adjustment. Adjust cassette latch.
l Faulty cassette sensor. Replace CR&P board.
l Inspect cassette for bent rollers.
l Inspect cassette for bent guide.
l Magnet missing from cassette.
Calibration Failed
l Ribbon installed incorrectly. Reinstall ribbon.
l Ribbon reel diameter is out of range. Use correct ribbon reel.
l Dirty ribbon calibration detector.
l Dirty emitter.
l Correct ribbon type.
433
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Ribbon Break
l Broken ribbon. Reinstall the ribbon.
l Too much slack in ribbon when cassette is loaded. Reinstall the ribbon.
l End of reel. Replace ribbon reel.
l Ribbon gets wrong side of peel roller when loading cassette. Reinstall the ribbon.
l Host machine grabbing ribbon with too much force. Review application and fix.
l Inspect friction on mandrels.
l Is the waste encoder readings?
l Inspect cassette for worn, bent or misaligned components.
l Correct ribbon settings.
Print quality problems
l Check condition of printhead.
l Check condition of platen.
l Check printhead force.
l Check darkness.
l Check print speed.
l Check gap from head to platen.
l Check printhead belt.
l Correct print mode.
l Correct print position.
l Correct print registration.
l Ribbon/substrate compatibility.
Calibration failure
l Are spools secure on hubs?
l Are cassette mandrels engaging fully?
l Check condition of drive belt.
l Sensors clean and undamaged?
l Are all motors being driven?
l Is the waste encoder reading?
Printer malfunctioning
l Use Diagnostics to isolate fault.
l Inputs status may be checked.
l Outputs may be toggled.
l Look for large ribbon tags.
434
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
False ribbon breaks
l Is correct ribbon width selected?
l Are ribbon cores secure on hubs?
l Is Ribbon correct type?
l Is Ribbon threaded correctly?
l Ensure ribbon is not being pulled by substrate.
Troubleshooting using the diagnostics page
To Access the Diagnostic Pages:
1. Press on the Tools button (near the top right of the screen).
2. Press on the Diagnostics panel; the following page will be displayed.
3. Select from the three areas, the area where the problem is suspected to exist.
If it is suspected that the printer is not correctly setting one of its outputs, e.g. the fault output is not being set correctly, this can
be tested by toggling the output.
1. First, the printer must be Offline.
2. Go to the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Outputs.
3. Press on the toggle button for the appropriate output.
The displayed state of the output will be seen to change. If the physical output does not change accordingly, then there could be
a problem with the output on the printer.
The Host Packaging Machine Won’t Run
If CLARiTY is displaying a Fault, then the printer will be activating its fault output. The fault output is intended to be wired to inter -
lock and stop the host packaging machine. Clear the fault see section ‘Clear a fault or warning’ to clear the fault. This will switch
the fault output and allow the host packaging machine to run again.
The state of the output can be examined on the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead Outputs.
This diagnostic should be checked to determine what the expected output of the printer should be.
Only Half of the Image Comes Out
l Check printhead mapping. The printhead 1 should be set to 1.
l Check the alignment of the printhead bracket.
l Ensure correct ribbon width selected.
l Ensure correct vertical registration value is selected.
The Print Signal is Connected, But It Doesn’t Print
View the diagnostics on the page Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Inputs.
The state of the print signal input that the printer is receiving is listed.
435
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Manually trigger the print sensor/signal, and check whether the diagnostic page shows a change in the state of the signal. If the
diagnostics do not show a change, then it is likely that the print signal is not being generated correctly.
Check all of the print signal/sensors connections. If the diagnostic page still does not show the expected read out, then change
the print signal/sensor.
The Print Appears Smeared
If the printer is working intermittently (the target material is stationary while printing is taking place), then the material may not
be coming to a complete rest before the print begins. Alter the Print Delay setting to correct the problem (see CLARITY Con -
figuration Manager: Set the Print Delay).
Ensuring Print Quality
Many things can cause poor print quality. This list provides methods for preventing and correcting common issues.
Ensure support bracket stability
Ensure the printer support is tightened and the printhead is level with the platen.
Figure 17- 1 on the facing page shows how print quality can be affected if the printer is uneven. An uneven printhead can cause
damage to the printhead or printer.
Uneven Printer Symptoms
l iAssure code failure
l Ribbon tracking failure
l Poor print quality
l Printhead damage
436
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 17- 1: Print from uneven Bracket.
Keep printer parallel to the substrate
The printer must be mounted evenly parallel to the substrate.
Printer not parallel to the substrate symptoms
l iAssure code failure
l Ribbon tracking failure
l Poor print quality
F IGURE 17- 2: Printer alignment on the support bracket.
437
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Keep the Platen clean
Ensure the platen is clean and free of wax and resin build ups. The rubber pad should be entirely adhered to the platen. iAssure
should indicate platen failures.
Dirty platen symptoms
l iAssure code failure
l Unprinted area
l Poor print quality
l Printhead damage
Possible causes of transfer of wax and resin to the platen:
l Print darkness could be too high
l Print speed could be too low
l Printing with no substrate present
Keep the Platen in Good Repair
Ensure the platen is smooth with no cuts or grooves.
Worn Platen Symptoms
l Obvious cuts or roves
l All prints exhibit unprinted lines in the same place. Note that this symptom can also be caused by ribbon
creases .
Gouges in the platen caused by:
l Normal wear and tear (ask customer how many prints)
l Print force pressure is too high.
438
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 17- 3: A worn Platen.
F IGURE 17- 4: Print with worn Platen.
Both printing on a worn Platen and ribbon wrinkle similarly result in lines in the print.
F IGURE 17- 5: Print from wrinkled ribbon.
Replace Worn Printhead Elements
Straight vertical lines are an indication of a dirty printhead or inactive printhead elements (dots).
Use Clarity Diagnostics to check for dead dots.
439
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Mitigating a Dead Dot
When only one element is worn out, you can move the print away from that dot by shifting the printer and
adjusting the registration.
F IGURE 17- 6: Print from dusty or damaged printhead.
Set the Correct Printhead Gap
l The Gap Tool allows a user to set the printer- to- platen gap.
l Use the Gap Tool to measure the distance/gap between the printhead and the print platen of a TTO printer. The ideal dis -
tance/gap between the print head and print platen is 2mm .
l Ensure the printer is off and in the home position while measuring.
l After the first print, the printhead gap value will display in the Diagnostics Screen on the controller.
l This parameter allows the printer to adjust its operation during printing, resulting in improved tension control.
F IGURE 17- 7: Printhead gap set with SAR 10222 5mm Gap Tool.
440
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
Set correct Vertical Registration Value
Confirm Vertical Registration is not set to a high value. Set values to 0 and check the print.
You can verify this setting in Clarity at Tools | Setup | Printhead.
In Figure 17- 8 below :
l The bottom print illustrates the vertical registration set to 0. Note the print is in the middle of the used ribbon. The position
of the print is based on were the print was created in the job.
l The middle print illustrates the vertical registration set to +20.
l The bottom print illustrates the vertical registration set to - 20.
F IGURE 17- 8: Vertical Registration examples.
Printer Settings Horizontal Registration
Confirm Horizontal Registration is not set to a high value. Set values to 0 and check for print.
F IGURE 17- 9: Horizontal Registration setting effects on print.
441
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Set correct Print Position
Print Position changes the angle of the printhead against the target material. This angle affects the print quality. If the angle is
not suitable, print may appear faded.
l For good print, position the printhead tip at 12 o'clock.
l Smeared or missed prints indicate that the printhead tip is too far back
l Ribbon tear indicates that the printhead tip is too far forward.
F IGURE 17- 10: Print Position setting effects on print.
Set correct Print Darkness
Incorrect darkness setting symptoms
l Printed image to appear faded (darkness is too low).
l iAssure code failure (print darkness is too low).
l The edges of the printed image appears blurred (print darkness is too high).
l High darkness settings at low print speeds (below 200) could cause ribbons to break and shortens the life of
the printhead.
l High darkness setting causes the ribbon to stick to the substrate which causes ribbon breaks and print quality
issues.
l High darkness values will overdrive the printhead and shorten its lifetime.
l Choose the lowest value of darkness that achieves a satisfactory quality print.
l Check darkness failure on iAssure
l A setting of 75- 85% will work for most of your applications.
442
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 17- 11: Low Darkness print result.
F IGURE 17- 12: High Darkness print result and broken ribbon.
443
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
Set correct Print Speed
Set Printhead Speed to default 200mm/s (Tools | Setup | Printhead).
l Lower the print speed when using Premium Ribbon .
l The thermal transfer ribbon used by the printer adheres more readily to some types of packaging materials than others.
l You can reduce Print Speed to improve bonding and print quality OR increase print speed to reduce the overall print cycle
time, thus achieving greater throughput in terms of packs per minute.
Intermittent Only
This feature can be applied only on intermittent mode applications.
l You must balance Print Darkness with Print Speed.
l Print Darkness and Print Speed settings are substrate dependent!
The faster your printing speed, the more incomplete your message will be. The print darkness must be increased accord -
ingly.
High Speed Printing
Please note that high speed printing is affected by gap, print speed, printhead force, darkness, and
(most importantly) substrate.
Prevent Over Printing
Over printing is caused by improperly winding the ribbon onto the cassette prior to inserting the cassette. Used ribbon is fed into
the printhead path and the printhead prints on used ribbon.
F IGURE 17- 13: Over Printing
444
Rev AD

Troubleshooting
Set Correct Trailing Edge Darkness
l The leading edge to the print is anywhere in a element row where the elements turns on, trailing edge is any distance after
that.
l In the direction of printing, the code goes from dark to light. As the printhead moves across the substrate the darkness
decreases.
The Trailing Edge Darkness setting controls the amount of energy sent to the printhead during the trailing edge of the print. Val -
ues can be 25 to 125.
If the match between the substrate, TTO printer, and ribbon is not optimised you may encounter print problems as illustrated in
Figure 17- 14 below .
F IGURE 17- 14: Trailing Edge Darkness at 25, 50, and 75 percent.

Glossary
A
AC
Alternating current
ACK
Acknowledge
B
Barcode
A barcode is an optical, machine- readable, representation of data; the data usually describes something about the object that carries
the barcode.
Baud rate
The speed that data is transmitted, and it is a derived value based on the number of symbols transmitted per second.
C
CIFF
Coder independent files.
CR
Carriage return
D
Data bits
The number of data bits in each character can be 5 (for Baudot code), 6 (rarely used), 7 (for true ASCII), 8 (for most kinds of data, as
this size matches the size of a byte), or 9 (rarely used). 8 data bits are almost universally used in newer applications. 5 or 7 bits gen -
erally only make sense with older equipment such as teleprinters.
DC
Direct current
465
Rev AD

6530 Student Training Guide
DGND
Digital ground
Dots
Printhead element
DPI
Dot per inch
E
Encoder
An encoder is a device, circuit, transducer, software program, algorithm or person that converts information from one format or code
to another, for the purposes of standardization, speed or compressions.
F
Field
A distinct item on an Image is called a Field. Each Field can be positioned relative to other fields on the Image (for example, text field,
data field and so on).
Flow control
A serial port may use signals in the interface to pause and resume the transmission of data. For example, a slow printer might need to
handshake with the serial port to indicate that data should be paused while the mechanism advances a line.
G
GUI
Graphical Unit Interface
H
Hand shaking
Gets or sets the handshaking protocol for serial port transmission of data using a value from Handshake.
HFFS
Horizontal form fill sealer

6530 Student Training Guide
LOGO
A graphic image used in CLARiSOFT software.
LPA
Label Print and Apply
M
Meter
The fundamental unit of length in the metric system, equal to 100 centimeters or approximately 39.37 inches.
MHz
Abbreviation for megahertz. One MHz represents one million cycles per second. The speed of microprocessors, called the clock speed, is
measured in megahertz.
micron
Also called micrometer. The millionth part of a meter. Symbol: μ, mu.
mm
Millimeter
N
NAK
Not acknowledge
O
OEE
Operation equipment efficiency
P
Parity
A parity bit, or check bit, is a bit added to a string of binary code to ensure that the total number of 1- bits in the string is even or odd.
Parity bits are used as the simplest form of error detecting code.

PNP
This is a sensor whose output pulls up to the positive supply rail when it senses a metal target. Thus any attached load to the sensor out -
put must be connected between zero volts & the output of the sensor to operate.
PSU

A relay is an electrically operated switch.

RX

SATO

Serial port
Serial port is a serial communication interface through which information transfers in or out one bit at a time.
